\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{tr}\Bigl(\beta^{-2}\left[\Diag(\mathrm{diag}(\Sigma)\right]\Bigr)+\mathrm{tr}\Bigl(\beta^{-2}\left[\Diag\left(\mathrm{diag}(\Sigma)\circ\mathrm{diag}(VV^{\top})\right)\right]\Bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}\,\mathrm{tr}(\beta^{-2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}},

where the second line follows from
4
and
1
. Hence, this term is absorbed within the remainder term
O_{p}\left(\tfrac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)
.
The plug–in estimator of
\mu_{1}
, defined in
Eq.

27
, is given by

\widehat{\mu}_{1}=\underbrace{2\tr\Bigl[\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\bigl(\widehat{\Sigma}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{\perp}\bigr)\Bigr]}_{\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{(1)}}+\underbrace{2\tr\Bigl[\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\Diag\bigl(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\widehat{d}\bigr)\Bigr]}_{\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{(2)}},

where all notation follows that of
Theorem

6
. To control the estimation error, we first consider the leading component
\mu_{1}^{(1)}
and decompose the difference as

\displaystyle\mu_{1}^{(1)}-\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\cdot\Sigma\circ\beta^{\perp}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma)-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma})-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\tr\left(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma})-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\cdot\widehat{\Sigma}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{\perp}\right).

An analogous decomposition applies to
\mu_{1}^{(2)}
:

\displaystyle\mu_{1}^{(2)}-\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma\cdot d)-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\tr\left(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\widehat{d})\right),

where
d=\mathrm{diag}\left(I-VV^{\top}\right)
and
\widehat{d}=\mathrm{diag}\left(I-\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}\right)
.

•

Bounding
|\mu_{1}^{(1)}-\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{(1)}|
. We first establish that

\displaystyle|\mu_{1}^{(1)}-\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{(1)}|=o_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right).

(50)

Consider

\displaystyle\big|\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Sigma\circ\beta^{\perp}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma)\right)\big|

\displaystyle=|\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Sigma\circ(VV^{\top})\right)|

(51)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\,|\tr\left(VV^{\top}\cdot\Sigma\circ(VV^{\top})\right)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\,\|VV^{\top}\|_{F}\,\|\Sigma\circ(VV^{\top})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\,\|VV^{\top}\|_{F}\,\|\Sigma\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{3/2}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}}

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right),

where the fourth line follows from
4
and the final line follows from
1
.

Next, we bound the term involving the deviation between
\Sigma
and
\widehat{\Sigma}
:

\displaystyle\big|\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma})\right)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(n\rho_{n})^{2}}\,\big|\tr\left(VV^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma})\right)\big|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right),

where the second line uses
Eq.

41
of
Lemma

14
.

We then control the eigenvalue and eigenvector perturbation component directly using
Eq.

42
of
Lemma

15
via

\displaystyle\big|\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma})-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma})\right)\big|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\log n}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right)\right)=o_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right).

Finally, for the term

\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma})-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\cdot\widehat{\Sigma}\circ\beta^{\perp}\right)=o_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right),

the same reasoning as in
Eq.

51
follows, with the incoherence of
\widehat{V}
(guaranteed by
Lemma

12
) replacing that of
V
.

Combining all four bounds above yields the desired rate in
Eq.

50
.

•

Bounding
|\mu_{1}^{(2)}-\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{(2)}|
.

We next establish that

\displaystyle|\mu_{1}^{(2)}-\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{(2)}|=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right).

(52)

Consider first

\displaystyle\big|\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma\cdot d)-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})\right)\big|

\displaystyle=\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma\cdot\mathrm{diag}(VV^{\top}))\right)

(53)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\tr\left(VV^{\top}\Diag(\Sigma\cdot\mathrm{diag}(VV^{\top}))\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)\tr\Big(\Diag(\Sigma\cdot\mathrm{diag}(VV^{\top}))\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\,\big(\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\cdot\Sigma\cdot\mathrm{diag}(VV^{\top})\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)\big(\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\cdot\Sigma\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{2}}(n^{2}\rho_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{k^{2}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}}

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right),

where we have used
4
and the final line follows from
1
.

Next, for the perturbation in
\Sigma
:

\displaystyle\big|\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(\Diag(\Sigma\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})-\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}))\right)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(n\rho_{n})^{2}}\big|\tr\left(VV^{\top}(\Diag(\Sigma\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})-\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}))\right)\big|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right),

where the last line follows from
Eq.

40
of
Lemma

14
.

We then control the eigenvalue and eigenvector perturbation component directly using
Eq.

43
of
Lemma

15
:

\displaystyle\Big|\tr\big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})\big)\Big|=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right).

Finally, for the term

\tr\left(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\widehat{d})\right),

we have the same argument as in
Eq.

53
, using incoherence of
\widehat{V}
which is guaranteed on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf very\ good}}
as per
Lemma

12
. Combining all four bounds yields the desired rate in
Eq.

52
.

Thus, from
Eq.

50
and
Eq.

52
we have

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}\big|=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)\right),

(54)

under
1
,
2′
,
3
and
4
.

Analyzing the Estimated Variance.

From
Eq.

26
, the population variance admits the expansion

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}^{2}=8\,\underbrace{\Bigl\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\beta^{-2}\circ\beta^{-2}),\Sigma^{2}\Bigr\rangle}_{(\sigma^{11})^{2}}+4\,\underbrace{\alpha^{\top}K\,\alpha}_{(\sigma^{12})^{2}}+o\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

The corresponding plug–in estimator, as defined in
Eq.

27
, takes the form

\widehat{\sigma}_{1}^{2}=8\,\underbrace{\Bigl\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{-2}),\widehat{\Sigma}^{2}\Bigr\rangle}_{(\widehat{\sigma}^{11})^{2}}+4\,\underbrace{\widehat{\alpha}^{\top}\widehat{K}\,\widehat{\alpha}}_{(\widehat{\sigma}^{12})^{2}},

where all notation follows that of
Theorem

6
. To analyze the estimation error, we first focus on the first component
\left(\sigma^{11}\right)^{2}
and decompose the difference as

\displaystyle(\widehat{\sigma}^{11})^{2}-(\sigma^{11})^{2}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\left\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{-2}),\,\widehat{\Sigma}^{2}-\Sigma^{2}\right\rangle}_{I_{1}}+\underbrace{\left\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{-2}-\beta^{-2}\circ\beta^{-2}),\,\Sigma^{2}\right\rangle}_{I_{2}}.

An analogous decomposition applies to
(\sigma^{12})^{2}
:

\displaystyle(\widehat{\sigma}^{12})^{2}-(\sigma^{12})^{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{\top}\widehat{K}\alpha-\widehat{\alpha}^{\top}K\widehat{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{\top}(\widehat{K}-K)\alpha+2\,\alpha^{\top}\widehat{K}(\widehat{\alpha}-\alpha)+(\widehat{\alpha}-\alpha)^{\top}\widehat{K}(\widehat{\alpha}-\alpha).

(55)

•

Bounding
|(\widehat{\sigma}^{11})^{2}-(\sigma^{11})^{2}|
.
We will establish that

\displaystyle\big|(\widehat{\sigma}^{11})^{2}-(\sigma^{11})^{2}\big|=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

(56)

Applying the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and noting that
\|(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ M\|_{F}\leq\|M\|_{F}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|I_{1}\big|

\displaystyle=\left\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{-2}),\,\widehat{\Sigma}^{2}-\Sigma^{2}\right\rangle

(57)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{-2})\right\|_{F}\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{2}-\Sigma^{2}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\right\|_{F}\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}^{2}-\Sigma^{2}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\right\|_{F}\left(\left\|\widehat{P}^{2}-P^{2}\right\|_{F}+2\left\|\widehat{P}({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})-P(P\circ P)\right\|_{F}+\left\|({\widehat{P}}\circ\widehat{P})^{2}-(P\circ P)^{2}\right\|_{F}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}\left\|((\widehat{V}^{(i)})(\widehat{V}^{(i)})^{\top})\circ((\widehat{V}^{(i)})(\widehat{V}^{(i)})^{\top})\right\|_{F}\left(\left\|\widehat{P}^{2}-P^{2}\right\|_{F}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{(k/n)}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}\,k^{1/2}(n\rho_{n})^{1.5}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{3/2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

Here, the third to last line and the second to last line follow from
Eqs.

47
,
48
and
49
in
Lemma

17
. The bound

\|((\widehat{V}^{(i)})(\widehat{V}^{(i)})^{\top})\circ((\widehat{V}^{(i)})(\widehat{V}^{(i)})^{\top})\|_{F}\asymp k/n
in the second to last line
is ensured by
4
.

Note the following bounds:

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}^{-2}+\beta^{-2}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\|_{\infty}+\|\beta^{-2}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right),

(58)

\displaystyle\|\Sigma^{2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Sigma^{2}-P^{2}\|_{F}+\|P^{2}\|_{F}

(59)

\displaystyle\leq\|(\Sigma-P)\Sigma+P(\Sigma-P)\|_{F}+\|P^{2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim n\rho_{n}\|\Sigma-P\|_{F}+kn^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim n\rho_{n}\|P\circ P\|_{F}+kn^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim n\rho_{n}^{2}\|P\|_{F}+kn^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim kn^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}.

Now, consider the term
I_{2}
. We have

\displaystyle\big|I_{2}\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\big\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}\circ\widehat{\beta}^{-2}-\beta^{-2}\circ\beta^{-2}),\,\Sigma^{2}\big\rangle\Big|

(60)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big|\big\langle(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}-\beta^{-2})\circ(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}+\beta^{-2}),\,\Sigma^{2}\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}-\beta^{-2})\circ(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}+\beta^{-2})\|_{F}\,\|\Sigma^{2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Sigma^{2}\|_{F}\,\|\widehat{\beta}^{-2}+\beta^{-2}\|_{\infty}\,\|\widehat{\beta}^{-2}-\beta^{-2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim kn^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}\cdot\|\widehat{\beta}^{-2}+\beta^{-2}\|_{\infty}\,\|\widehat{\beta}^{-2}-\beta^{-2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{3/2}}{n^{7/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{3/2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

The third to last line and the second to last line follow from
Eq.

46
in
Lemma

16
and
Eqs.

58
and
59
.
Combining
Eqs.

57
and
60
yields
Eq.

56
.

•

Bounding
|(\widehat{\sigma}^{12})^{2}-(\sigma^{12})^{2}|
.
We will now establish that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}_{12}^{2}-\sigma_{12}^{2}|=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

(61)

Recalling that
\alpha=\mathrm{diag}(\beta^{-2})
, we have that

\|\alpha\|_{\infty}\asymp\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},
and

\|\alpha\|_{2}\asymp\frac{k}{n^{2.5}\rho_{n}^{2}}.
For the first term on the right hand side of
Eq.

55
, we have

\displaystyle|{\alpha}^{\top}(\widehat{K}-K){\alpha}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\alpha\|^{2}\cdot\|\widehat{K}-K\|

(62)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{5}\rho_{n}^{4}}\right)\cdot\|P-{\widehat{P}}\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4.5}\rho_{n}^{3.5}}\right)=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right),

which holds on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf good}}
as explained in
Lemma

1
. To establish the bound
\|\widehat{K}-K\|\lesssim\|P-{\widehat{P}}\|
, we first observe that

\|\widehat{K}-K\|\lesssim\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|+\|\widehat{\Sigma}\circ\widehat{P}-\Sigma\circ P\|.

By the triangle inequality, the second term on the right-hand side satisfies

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{\Sigma}\circ\widehat{P}-\Sigma\circ P\big\|\leq\big\|\widehat{\Sigma}\circ(\widehat{P}-P)\big\|+\big\|P\circ(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\big\|.

(63)

For the second term on the right hand side of
Eq.

63
,
4
,
3
and
1
imply

\displaystyle\big\|P\circ(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\big\|

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\sum_{l=1}^{k}\lambda_{l}\Diag(V_{l.})(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma)\Diag(V_{l.})\bigg\|

(64)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{l=1}^{k}\lambda_{l}\|V_{l.}\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)\big\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\big\|

\displaystyle\leq k^{2}\rho_{n}\big\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\big\|\ll\big\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\big\|.

Similarly, the first term on the right hand side of
Eq.

63
can be bounded as

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{\Sigma}\circ(\widehat{P}-P)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\widehat{P}\circ(\widehat{P}-P)\big\|+\big\|(\widehat{P}\circ\widehat{P})\circ(\widehat{P}-P)\big\|

(65)

\displaystyle\ll\big\|\widehat{P}-P\big\|

with probability
1-O(n^{-19})
, where the final step follows from
Lemma

12
,
3
and
1
, and the fact that
\rank(\widehat{P}\circ\widehat{P})\leq k^{2}
. Under the same assumptions, an analogous argument yields
\|\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma\|\lesssim\|P-{\widehat{P}}\|
. Combining this with
Eq.

64
and
Eq.

65
establishes the desired bound
\|\widehat{K}-K\|\lesssim\|P-{\widehat{P}}\|
.

For the cross term in
Eq.

55
, it follows that

\displaystyle|{\alpha}^{\top}\widehat{K}(\widehat{\alpha}-{\alpha})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\alpha\|\,\|\widehat{K}\|\,\|\widehat{\alpha}-{\alpha}\|

(66)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4.5}\rho_{n}^{3.5}}\right)=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right),

where the second line uses
Eq.

44
of
Lemma

16
.

For the quadratic term in
Eq.

55
, again by
Eq.

44
,

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\alpha}-{\alpha})^{\top}\widehat{K}(\widehat{\alpha}-{\alpha})|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{K}\|\cdot\|\widehat{\alpha}-{\alpha}\|^{2}

(67)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(n\rho_{n}\cdot\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2.5}}\right)^{2}\right)=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

Combining
Eqs.

62
,
66
,
67
and
55
yields

\displaystyle\big|(\widehat{\sigma}^{12})^{2}-(\sigma^{12})^{2}\big|=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}{\rho_{n}}^{2}}\right).

(68)

Consequently,
Eq.

68
and
Eq.

56
imply that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}_{1}^{2}-\sigma_{1}^{2}|=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

(69)

Lemma

3
implies that
\widehat{\sigma}_{1}^{2}\asymp\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}
. Combining this with
Eq.

69
, we obtain

\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{1}}{\sigma_{1}}\xrightarrow{p}1.

Furthermore,
2′
,
Eq.

54
and
Lemma

3
imply that

\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{1}-\mu_{1}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{1}}\xrightarrow{p}0,

which verifies the conditions required for the consistency of the estimator.
∎

C.3.2
Proof of
Theorem

3

We will next establish
Theorem

3
.

Proof.

Computing the mean
. The population mean for the two–sample statistic admits the decomposition

\mu_{2}=2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\mu_{2}^{(i)}=2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tr\Bigl[\beta_{i}^{-2}\Bigl({\Sigma^{(i)}}\circ\beta_{i}^{\perp}+\Diag({\Sigma^{(i)}}\cdot d_{i})\Bigr)\Bigr],

where
\mu_{2}^{(i)}:=\tr\Bigl[\beta_{i}^{-2}\Bigl({\Sigma^{(i)}}\circ\beta_{i}^{\perp}+\Diag({\Sigma^{(i)}}\cdot d_{i})\Bigr)\Bigr].

The corresponding plug–in estimator introduced in
Eq.

20
is

\widehat{\mu}_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\widehat{\mu}_{2}^{(i)}=2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tr\Bigl[\widehat{\beta}_{i}^{-2}\Bigl({\widehat{P}^{(i)}}\circ(J_{n}-{\widehat{P}^{(i)}})\circ\widehat{\beta}_{i}^{\perp}+\Diag\bigl(({\widehat{P}^{(i)}}\circ(J_{n}-{\widehat{P}^{(i)}}))\cdot\widehat{d}_{i}\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr],

with notation as in
Theorem

3
.

Since the two–sample mean decomposes as the sum of the two one–sample contributions, the consistency and rate established in
Theorem

6
apply componentwise. In particular, for each
i\in\{1,2\}
we have

\big|\widehat{\mu}_{2}^{(i)}-\mu_{2}^{(i)}\big|=O_{p}\Bigl(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\Bigr),

and hence

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{\mu}_{2}-\mu_{2}\big|=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right)\right),

(70)

which establishes the claimed mean consistency for the two–sample statistic and thus proves the first part of
Theorem

3
.

Computing the Variance
. From
Theorem

2
, the population variance for the two–sample statistic admits the decomposition

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigl(\sigma_{2}^{(i)}\bigr)^{2}+\sigma_{2}^{(1,2)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\!\left(8\,\bigl\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\!\circ\!(\beta_{i}^{-2}\!\circ\!\beta_{i}^{-2}),\,({\Sigma^{(i)}})^{2}\bigr\rangle+4\,\alpha_{i}^{\top}K^{(i)}\,\alpha_{i}\right)}_{\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\sigma_{2}^{(i)})^{2}}+\underbrace{4\,\bigl\langle G\!\circ\!G,{\Sigma^{(1)}}^{\top}{\Sigma^{(2)}}+(J_{n}-I_{n})\!\circ\!({\Sigma^{(1)}}\!\circ\!{\Sigma^{(2)}})\bigr\rangle}_{\sigma_{2}^{(1,2)}}.

The corresponding plug–in estimator introduced in
Eq.

20
takes the form

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bigl(\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{(i)}\bigr)^{2}+\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{(1,2)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\begin{aligned} \sum_{i=1}^{2}\biggl[\,&8\,\bigl\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\widehat{\beta}_{i}^{-2}\circ\widehat{\beta}_{i}^{-2}),\,(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(i)})^{2}\bigr\rangle+4\,\widehat{\alpha}_{i}^{\top}{\widehat{K}^{(i)}}\,\widehat{\alpha}_{i}\biggr]\end{aligned}}_{\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{(i)})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\begin{aligned} 4\,\bigl\langle\widehat{G}\circ\widehat{G},(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})+(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ((\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\circ(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))\bigr\rangle\end{aligned}}_{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{(1,2)}}

with notation as in
Theorem

3
.

From the one–sample variance consistency result as in
Eq.

69
, it follows that

|(\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{(i)})^{2}-(\sigma_{2}^{(i)})^{2}|\ll\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},
for
i=1,2
.
To establish the full variance consistency of the two–sample statistic, it remains to verify that

|\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{(1,2)}-\sigma_{2}^{(1,2)}|=\;o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).
We decompose the difference as

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{(1,2)}-\sigma_{2}^{(1,2)}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\widehat{G}\!\circ\!\widehat{G},\;(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})+(J_{n}-I_{n})\!\circ\!((\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))\Big\rangle-\Big\langle G\!\circ\!G,\;{\Sigma^{(1)}}^{\top}{\Sigma^{(2)}}+(J_{n}-I_{n})\!\circ\!({\Sigma^{(1)}}\!\circ\!{\Sigma^{(2)}})\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\Big\langle\widehat{G}\!\circ\!\widehat{G}-G\circ G,\;(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})+(J_{n}-I_{n})\!\circ\!((\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))\Big\rangle}_{Q_{2}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\Big\langle G\!\circ\!G,\;({\Sigma^{(1)}}^{\top}{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))+(J_{n}-I_{n})\!\circ\!({\Sigma^{(1)}}\!\circ\!{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))\Big\rangle}_{Q_{1}}.

We first bound
Q_{2}
. Observe that

\displaystyle\big|\langle\widehat{G}\!\circ\!\widehat{G}-G\circ G,\;(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})+(J_{n}-I_{n})\!\circ\!((\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\big|\langle\widehat{G}\!\circ\!\widehat{G}-G\circ G,\;(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})\rangle\big|

(71)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\widehat{G}\!\circ\!\widehat{G}-G\circ G\|_{F}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{k}\|\widehat{G}\!\circ\!\widehat{G}-G\circ G\|_{F}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\|_{2}\|^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{k}\cdot\frac{k^{3/2}}{n^{5.5}\rho_{n}^{4.5}}\cdot n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3.5}\rho_{n}^{2.5}}\right)

\displaystyle=o_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right),

where the fourth line follows from
Eq.

45
of
Lemma

16
.

Next, we bound
Q_{1}
. Observe that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\big|\langle G\!\circ\!G,\;({\Sigma^{(1)}}{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))+(J_{n}-I_{n})\!\circ\!({\Sigma^{(1)}}\!\circ\!{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))\rangle\big|

(72)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\big|\langle G\!\circ\!G,\;{\Sigma^{(1)}}{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})\rangle\big|+\big|\langle G\!\circ\!G,({\Sigma^{(1)}}\!\circ\!{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))\rangle\big|.

By Hölder’s inequality for Frobenius inner products,

\big|\langle G\!\circ\!G,\;{\Sigma^{(1)}}{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})\rangle\big|\leq\|G\!\circ\!G\|_{F}\,\|{\widehat{P}^{(1)}}{\widehat{P}^{(2)}}-{P^{(1)}}{P^{(2)}}\|_{F}.

Using the identity

{\widehat{P}^{(1)}}{\widehat{P}^{(2)}}-{P^{(1)}}{P^{(2)}}={\widehat{P}^{(1)}}({\widehat{P}^{(2)}}-{P^{(2)}})-({\widehat{P}^{(1)}}-{P^{(1)}}){P^{(2)}},

together with
\|G\!\circ\!G\|_{F}\leq\|G\|_{\infty}\|G\|_{F}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|G\!\circ\!G\|_{F}\,\|{\widehat{P}^{(1)}}{\widehat{P}^{(2)}}-{P^{(1)}}{P^{(2)}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|G\|_{\infty}\|G\|_{F}\Big(\|{\widehat{P}^{(1)}}\|_{F}\|{X^{(2)}}\|+\|{X^{(1)}}\|\|{P^{(2)}}\|_{F}\Big).

(73)

Invoking
1
,
3
and
4
and
Lemma

12
, we have

\|G\|_{\infty}\leq\|\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}+\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},\qquad\|G\|_{F}\leq\|\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}+\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}\leq\frac{\sqrt{k}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}.

Thus,
Eq.

73
is bounded by

\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{k}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\Big(\|{\widehat{P}^{(1)}}\|_{F}\|{X^{(2)}}\|+\|{X^{(1)}}\|\|{P^{(2)}}\|_{F}\Big)=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3.5}\rho_{n}^{2.5}}\right)=o_{p}\!\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right),

which completes the bound for the first term on the right hand side of
Eq.

72
.
Before bounding the second term on the right hand side of
Eq.

72
, we observe that
({\Sigma^{(1)}}\!\circ\!{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))
is an
n\times n
matrix whose elements are
O(\rho_{n}^{2}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\big|\langle G\!\circ\!G,\;({\Sigma^{(1)}}\!\circ\!{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|G\!\circ\!G\|_{F}\|{\Sigma^{(1)}}\!\circ\!{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)})\|_{F}

(74)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{3/2}}{n^{5}\rho_{n}^{4}}\cdot\sqrt{k}n\rho_{n}\right)=o_{p}\!\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

Thus,
Eqs.

74
and
72
give the following bound on
Q_{1}

\big|\langle G\!\circ\!G,\;({\Sigma^{(1)}}{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))+(J_{n}-I_{n})\!\circ\!({\Sigma^{(1)}}\!\circ\!{\Sigma^{(2)}}-(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(1)})\!\circ\!(\widehat{\Sigma}^{(2)}))\rangle\big|=o_{p}\!\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

Combining it with
Eq.

71
where we bounded
Q_{2}
, we conclude that

\big|\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{(1,2)}-\sigma_{2}^{(1,2)}\big|\ll\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},
implying

\displaystyle\big|\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{2}-\sigma_{2}^{2}\big|\ll\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}.

(75)

Lemma

8
implies that
\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{2}\asymp\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}
. Combining this with
Eq.

75
, we obtain

\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}{\sigma_{2}}\xrightarrow{p}1.

Furthermore,
2′
,
Eq.

70
and
Lemma

8
imply that

\frac{\widehat{\mu}_{2}-\mu_{2}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\xrightarrow{p}0,

which completes the proof of
Theorem

3
.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs of Corollaries from
Section

2.3
and
Theorem

4

We first state some lemmas which will be used to prove the corollaries for the consistency of our test.

Auxiliary Lemmas for Consistency of Our Test

Lemma 18

(Cross–term moments)
.

Suppose
1
,
3
,
4
and
2′
hold. Then

\frac{\langle{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top},\;\widehat{V^{(2)}}\widehat{V^{(2)}}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\rangle}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\asymp 1,

where
\widehat{\sigma}_{2}
is the variance estimator defined in
Eq.

20
.

Proof.

See
Section

H.1
.
∎

Lemma 19
.

Suppose
1
,
3
,
4
and
2′
hold. Let
\Delta={V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}.
Then the quantity

L(\Delta):=\big\langle(\widehat{V}^{(1)})(\widehat{V}^{(1)})^{\top}-{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top},\;\Delta\big\rangle-\big\langle(\widehat{V}^{(2)})(\widehat{V}^{(2)})^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top},\Delta\big\rangle,

satisfies

L(\Delta)=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{\|\Delta\|_{F}}{n\rho_{n}},\frac{\|\Delta\|_{F}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n\rho_{n}}\right)\right).

Proof.

See
Section

H.2
.
∎

Lemma 20

(Balanced communities imply incoherence)
.

Let
P=ZBZ^{\top}
denote the population probability matrix of a SBM, where
Z\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
is the membership matrix with exactly one unit entry per row and
B\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
is a symmetric block matrix. Let
n_{a}
denote the size of community
a
and set
N=\mathrm{diag}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=Z^{\top}Z
. Assume
\rank(P)=k
. If the community sizes are balanced in the sense that

n_{a}\asymp\frac{n}{k},

then
4
holds.

Proof.

See
Section

H.3
.
∎

Lemma 21

(Balanced mixed memberships imply incoherence)
.

Let
P=ZBZ^{\top}
denote the population probability matrix of a MMSBM, where
Z\in[0,1]^{n\times k}
has rows
{Z^{(i)}}^{\top}
lying in the probability simplex (
\sum_{a=1}^{k}z_{i,a}=1
for each
i
),
B\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
is symmetric, and
\rank(P)=k
. Define the Gram matrix
N:=Z^{\top}Z\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
and let
V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
be an orthonormal basis of
\col(P)
(the population eigenvectors associated to the nonzero eigenvalues). Suppose there exist constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
(independent of
n
) such that

\lambda_{\min}(N)\geq c_{1}\,\frac{n}{k}
.
Then
4
holds.

Proof.

See
Section

H.4
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

4

Proof.

We begin by decomposing the test statistic in terms of its population and estimation components. Using the expression of
\widehat{\mu}_{2}
from
Eq.

20
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\|\widehat{V}_{1}\widehat{V}_{1}^{\top}-\widehat{V}_{2}\widehat{V}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}-\widehat{\mu}_{2}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|\widehat{V}_{1}\widehat{V}_{1}^{\top}-{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}-\widehat{\mu}_{2}^{(1)}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}+\frac{\|\widehat{V}_{2}\widehat{V}_{2}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}-\widehat{\mu}_{2}^{(2)}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}

(76)

\displaystyle+\frac{\|{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}-2\,\frac{\langle\widehat{V}_{1}\widehat{V}_{1}^{\top}-{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top},\,\widehat{V}_{2}\widehat{V}_{2}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\rangle}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}+2\,\frac{L(\Delta)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}},

where
L(\Delta)
is defined in
Lemma

19
.
From
Theorem

5
, the first two terms on the right hand side of
Eq.

76

\frac{\|\widehat{V}_{i}\widehat{V}_{i}^{\top}-{V^{(i)}}{V^{(i)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}-\widehat{\mu}_{2}^{(i)}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\asymp 1

for
i=1,2
. Furthermore, by
Lemma

18
, the cross–sample interaction term satisfies

\frac{\langle\widehat{V}_{1}\widehat{V}_{1}^{\top}-{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top},\,\widehat{V}_{2}\widehat{V}_{2}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\rangle}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\asymp 1.

Turning to the component involving
L(\Delta)
,
Lemma

19
yields

L(\Delta)=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{\|\Delta\|_{F}}{n\rho_{n}},\frac{\|\Delta\|_{F}\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}}{n\rho_{n}}\right)\right).

Finally, recalling from
Lemma

8
that
\widehat{\sigma}_{2}\asymp\dfrac{k}{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}}
, the decomposition in
Eq.

76
reduces to the asymptotic expansion

\frac{\|\widehat{V}_{1}\widehat{V}_{1}^{\top}-\widehat{V}_{2}\widehat{V}_{2}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}-\widehat{\mu}_{2}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\asymp\zeta_{1}+\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}}{k}\,\|\Delta\|_{F}^{2}+\zeta_{2},

(77)

where
\zeta_{1}\asymp 1
and
\zeta_{2}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\|\Delta\|_{F}}{k},\frac{\|\Delta\|_{F}\sqrt{n\rho_{n}\log n}}{k}\right)\right)
.

Therefore, for the left–hand side of
Eq.

77
to diverge it suffices that
\dfrac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}}{k}\|\Delta\|_{F}^{2}
diverges; equivalently,

\|\Delta\|_{F}^{2}\gg\frac{k}{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}}.
We note that forcing divergence via
\zeta_{2}
would require
\|\Delta\|_{F}^{2}\gg\min\left(\dfrac{k^{2}}{n},\dfrac{k^{2}}{n\rho_{n}\log n}\right)
and under
2′
, we have
\min\left(\dfrac{k^{2}}{n},\dfrac{k^{2}}{n\rho_{n}\log n}\right)\gg\dfrac{k}{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}}
.

Thus, in the dense regime (
2′
) the test attains power tending to one whenever

\|{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\gg\frac{k}{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}},

which verifies the signal–strength condition stated in
Eq.

22
and completes the argument.
∎

Proof of
Corollary

1

Proof.

Using Lemma 1 from
1
, in the SBM setting with balanced communities, the population eigenspace projector admits the explicit representation

Q_{i}={V^{(i)}}{V^{(i)}}^{\top}={Z^{(i)}}({Z^{(i)}}^{\top}{Z^{(i)}})^{-1}{Z^{(i)}}^{\top}=\sum_{l=1}^{k}\frac{1}{{n^{(i)}_{l}}}\,({Z^{(i)}})_{\cdot l}({Z^{(i)}})_{\cdot l}^{\top}
for
i=1,2
,
where
{n^{(i)}_{l}}=n{\pi^{(i)}_{l}}
denotes the size of community
l
under model
i
. Since each
Q_{i}
is an orthogonal projector of rank
k
, we may write

\displaystyle\|Q_{1}-Q_{2}\|_{F}^{2}=\tr(Q_{1})+\tr(Q_{2})-2\tr(Q_{1}Q_{2})=2k-2\tr(Q_{1}Q_{2}).

(78)

Hence it suffices to analyse
\tr(Q_{1}Q_{2})
using the block expansions above. Under the balanced–community assumption (so that
{\pi^{(i)}_{l}}
are bounded away from
0
and
\infty
) we have

\displaystyle\tr(Q_{1}Q_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}=1}^{k}\frac{((Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l_{1}}^{\top}(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l_{2}})^{2}}{{n^{(1)}_{l}}{n^{(2)}_{l}}}

(79)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}=1}^{k}\frac{((Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l_{1}}^{\top}(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l_{2}})^{2}}{{\pi_{l_{1}}^{(1)}}{\pi_{l_{2}}^{(2)}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\frac{((Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}^{\top}(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l})^{2}}{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}+O\left(\frac{\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{0}^{2}}{n^{2}}\right).

The last big-O term arises from the fact that
((Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l_{1}}^{\top}(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l_{2}})^{2}\lesssim\|(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l_{1}}-(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l_{2}}\|_{0}^{2}
if
l_{1}\neq l_{2}
and corresponds to the cross term contributions. These cross-terms count nodes that moved from community
l_{1}
to community
l_{2}
.

Let
w_{l}:=(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}-(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l}
. Then

\|w_{l}\|^{2}=(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}^{\top}(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}+(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l}^{\top}(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l}-2\,(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}^{\top}(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l}=n({\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}})-2\,(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}^{\top}(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l},

so that

(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}^{\top}(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l}=\frac{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}{2}\,n-\tfrac{1}{2}\|w_{l}\|^{2}.

Squaring gives the expansion

((Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}^{\top}(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l})^{2}=\Big(\frac{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}{2}\Big)^{2}n^{2}-\Big(\frac{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}{2}\Big)n\,\|w_{l}\|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{4}\|w_{l}\|^{4}.

Substituting this into the expression for
\tr(Q_{1}Q_{2})
in
Eq.

79
and using
{n^{(i)}_{l}}=n{\pi^{(i)}_{l}}
yields

\displaystyle\tr(Q_{1}Q_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{k}\frac{\Big(\frac{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}{2}\Big)^{2}}{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\frac{-\Big(\frac{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}{2}\Big)n\,\|w_{l}\|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{4}\|w_{l}\|^{4}}{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}+O\left(\frac{\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{0}^{2}}{n^{2}}\right).

\displaystyle=k+\sum_{l=1}^{k}\frac{({\pi^{(1)}_{l}}-{\pi^{(2)}_{l}})^{2}}{4{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}+\sum_{l=1}^{k}\Bigg[-\frac{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}{2n{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}\,\|w_{l}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{4n^{2}{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}\,\|w_{l}\|^{4}\Bigg]+O\left(\frac{\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{0}^{2}}{n^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=k+\sum_{l=1}^{k}\Bigg[-\frac{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}{2n{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}\,\|w_{l}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{4n^{2}{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}\,\|w_{l}\|^{4}\Bigg]+O\left(\frac{\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{0}^{2}}{n^{2}}\right).

The leading
k
–term of the above expression cancels with the
2k
appearing in
Eq.

78
. Hence

\displaystyle\|Q_{1}-Q_{2}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=2k-2\tr(Q_{1}Q_{2})

(80)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{l=1}^{k}\Bigg[\frac{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}{2n{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}\,\|w_{l}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{4n^{2}{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}\,\|w_{l}\|^{4}\Bigg]+O\left(\frac{\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{0}^{2}}{n^{2}}\right).

Now, if
\frac{\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{0}^{2}}{n^{2}}\asymp 1
, then the condition
\max_{1\leq l\leq k}\sum_{j=1}^{n}|Z_{jl}^{(1)}-Z_{jl}^{(2)}|\gtrsim\frac{k}{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}}
is already satisfied trivially. So, we will assume
\frac{\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{0}}{n}\ll 1
.
From the definition of
w_{l}
, we have

\displaystyle\|w_{l}\|^{2}\leq n_{l}^{(1)}+n_{l}^{(2)}=n{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+n{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}=n({\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}})

which implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4n^{2}{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}\,\|w_{l}\|^{4}\lesssim\frac{{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}+{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}{2n{\pi^{(1)}_{l}}{\pi^{(2)}_{l}}}\,\|w_{l}\|^{2}

under the balanced–community assumption that
{\pi^{(i)}_{l}}
are bounded above and below by constants. Thus, discarding the smaller quartic correction and the big-O term gives

\|Q_{1}-Q_{2}\|_{F}^{2}\asymp\frac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^{k}\|(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}-(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l}\|^{2}.

Combining this representation with the condition from
Theorem

4
,

\|Q_{1}-Q_{2}\|_{F}^{2}\gg\frac{k}{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}},

we obtain the necessary lower bound

\sum_{l=1}^{k}\|(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}-(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l}\|^{2}\gg\frac{k}{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}},

and in particular since community sizes are balanced,

\max_{1\leq l\leq k}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left|Z_{jl}^{(1)}-Z_{jl}^{(2)}\right|\gg\frac{k}{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}},

and each
\|(Z^{(1)})_{\cdot l}-(Z^{(2)})_{\cdot l}\|^{2}
equals twice the Hamming distance for binary indicator vectors. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Corollary

2

Proof.

Adopting the notation from the statement, for
i=1,2
write

Q_{i}={V^{(i)}}{V^{(i)}}^{\top}={Z^{(i)}}({Z^{(i)}}^{\top}{Z^{(i)}})^{-1}{Z^{(i)}}^{\top},

where the second equality follows from the mixed–membership representation
{V^{(i)}}={Z^{(i)}}({Z^{(i)}}^{\top}{Z^{(i)}})^{-1/2}T
with
T
orthogonal.

We begin with the decomposition

\displaystyle\|Q_{1}-Q_{2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|Z^{(1)}({Z^{(1)}}^{\top}Z^{(1)})^{-1}{Z^{(1)}}^{\top}-Z^{(2)}({Z^{(2)}}^{\top}Z^{(2)})^{-1}{Z^{(2)}}^{\top}\|_{F}

(81)

\displaystyle\leq\|(Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)})({Z^{(1)}}^{\top}Z^{(1)})^{-1}{Z^{(1)}}^{\top}\|_{F}+\|Z^{(2)}\bigl(({Z^{(1)}}^{\top}Z^{(1)})^{-1}-({Z^{(2)}}^{\top}Z^{(2)})^{-1}\bigr){Z^{(1)}}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle+\|Z^{(2)}({Z^{(2)}}^{\top}Z^{(2)})^{-1}(Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)})^{\top}\|_{F}.

Under the spectral gap hypothesis
\lambda_{\min}({Z^{(i)}}^{\top}{Z^{(i)}})\geq c\,\frac{n}{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\|({Z^{(i)}}^{\top}{Z^{(i)}})^{-1}\|\lesssim\frac{k}{n}\quad\text{and}\quad\|{Z^{(i)}}\|_{F}\lesssim\sqrt{n}

(82)

for
i=1,2.

Hence the first and third terms on the right hand side of
Eq.

81
are immediately bounded by

\displaystyle\|(Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)})({Z^{(1)}}^{\top}Z^{(1)})^{-1}{Z^{(1)}}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k\,\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{n}},

\displaystyle\|Z^{(2)}({Z^{(2)}}^{\top}Z^{(2)})^{-1}(Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)})^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k\,\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{n}}.

To handle the middle term in (
81
) set
A={Z^{(1)}}^{\top}Z^{(1)}
and
B={Z^{(2)}}^{\top}Z^{(2)}
. Using the identity
A^{-1}-B^{-1}=A^{-1}(B-A)B^{-1}
we obtain

\displaystyle\|Z^{(2)}(A^{-1}-B^{-1}){Z^{(1)}}^{\top}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|Z^{(2)}\|_{F}\,\|A^{-1}(B-A)B^{-1}\|_{F}\,\|Z^{(1)}\|_{F}

(83)

\displaystyle\leq\|Z^{(2)}\|_{F}\|A^{-1}\|\|B-A\|_{F}\|B^{-1}\|\|Z^{(1)}\|_{F}.

A direct expansion gives

\displaystyle\|B-A\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|{Z^{(2)}}^{\top}Z^{(2)}-{Z^{(1)}}^{\top}Z^{(1)}\|_{F}=\|{Z^{(2)}}^{\top}(Z^{(2)}-Z^{(1)})\|_{F}+\|(Z^{(2)}-Z^{(1)})^{\top}Z^{(1)}\|_{F}

(84)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|Z^{(2)}\|_{F}\|Z^{(2)}-Z^{(1)}\|_{F}+\|Z^{(1)}\|_{F}\|Z^{(2)}-Z^{(1)}\|_{F}\lesssim\sqrt{n}\,\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{F}.

Combining
Eq.

84
with
Eqs.

83
and
82
yields

\|Z^{(2)}(A^{-1}-B^{-1}){Z^{(1)}}^{\top}\|_{F}\lesssim\frac{k^{2}\,\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{F}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Collecting the bounds for the three terms on the right hand side of
Eq.

81
we obtain
\|Q_{1}-Q_{2}\|_{F}\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\,\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{F}.

Recalling the signal requirement from
Theorem

4
,

\|Q_{1}-Q_{2}\|_{F}^{2}\gg\frac{k}{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}},

and substituting the preceding inequality gives the condition on the membership matrices

\|Z^{(1)}-Z^{(2)}\|_{F}^{2}\gg\frac{1}{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}},

as claimed.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs of Lemmas from
Appendix

A

In this section we prove all of the technical results stated in
Appendix

A
.

Proof of
Lemma

1

Proof.

The probability bound for
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf good}}
follows from a direct application of Corollary 3.12 of
10
.
Specifically, for a random matrix
X=A-P
with independent, mean-zero entries, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\|X\|\geq(1+\epsilon)\,2\widetilde{\sigma}+t\right)\leq\exp\left(\log n-\frac{t^{2}}{c_{\epsilon}^{2}\,\widetilde{\sigma}_{*}^{\,2}}\right),

where
\widetilde{\sigma}=\max_{i}\sqrt{\sum_{j}\mathbb{E}({X_{ij}}^{2})}
and

\widetilde{\sigma}_{*}=\max_{i,j}\|X_{ij}\|_{\infty}
.
In our setting, since the entries of
A
are Bernoulli with mean
P_{ij}\asymp\rho_{n}
, we have

\widetilde{\sigma}\asymp\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}
and
\widetilde{\sigma}_{*}\leq 1
.
Choosing
t=\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}
gives

\mathbb{P}\left(\|X\|\gtrsim\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}\right)\leq\exp\left(\log n-\frac{n\rho_{n}}{C^{2}}\right),

for some universal constant
C>0
.
Under
1
and
2
, the right-hand side can be shown as
O(n^{-19})
.

The Davis–Kahan perturbation bound on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf good}}
follows from Corollary 2.8 of
21
.
Since
\|X\|\lesssim\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}
on
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf good}}
,
and by
3
we have

\|X\|<(1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}})\lambda_{k}
,
the eigengap condition required by Davis–Kahan holds.
Consequently,

\|\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim\frac{k\,\|X\|^{2}}{(n\rho_{n})^{2}}.

Substituting
\|X\|\lesssim\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}
yields the desired bound

\big\|\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\big\|_{F}^{2}\lesssim\frac{k}{n\rho_{n}},

which holds on the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf good}}
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

2

Proof.

Define

M(X)=X^{\top}X,\qquad M(Y)=Y^{\top}Y,

where
Y=\beta^{\perp}X
. It therefore suffices to analyze

\frac{\Var\big(\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}-\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\big)}{\Var\big(\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\big)}=\frac{\Var\big(\tr\big(\beta^{-1}(M(Y)-M(X))\beta^{-1}\big)\big)}{\Var\big(\tr\big(\beta^{-1}M(X)\beta^{-1}\big)\big)}.

We can write

M(X)-M(Y)=X^{\top}X-X^{\top}\beta^{\perp}\beta^{\perp}X=X^{\top}(I-\beta^{\perp})X=X^{\top}VV^{\top}X.

Denote the inner matrix by
W=VV^{\top}
. Then

\Var\left(\tr\big(\beta^{-1}(M(Y)-M(X))\beta^{-1}\big)\right)=\Var\left(\tr\left(VV^{\top}X\beta^{-2}X^{\top}\right)\right).

Hence, to demonstrate that
\Var\big(\tr(\beta^{-1}(M(Y)-M(X))\beta^{-1})\big)
is asymptotically negligible compared to
\Var\big(\tr(\beta^{-1}M(X)\beta^{-1})\big)
, it suffices to establish that

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(VV^{\top}X\beta^{-2}X^{\top}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(X\beta^{-2}X^{\top}\right)\right)}\to 0.

The trace appearing in the numerator can be expanded as

\tr\big(VV^{\top}X\beta^{-2}X\big)=\sum_{a,b,c}(VV^{\top})_{a,b}\,(\beta^{-2})_{c,c}\,X_{a,c}X_{b,c}.

We partition the above sum into diagonal (
a=b
) and off-diagonal (
a\neq b
) terms. For the diagonal terms (
a=b
),

\displaystyle\Var\bigg(\sum_{a,c}(VV^{\top})_{a,a}(\beta^{-2})_{c,c}X_{a,c}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a,c}(VV^{\top})_{a,a}^{2}(\beta^{-2})_{c,c}^{2}\Var(X_{a,c}^{2})

(85)

\displaystyle\asymp\rho_{n}\sum_{a,c}(VV^{\top})_{a,a}^{2}(\beta^{-2})_{c,c}^{2}.

For the off-diagonal terms (
a\neq b
),

\displaystyle\Var\bigg(\sum_{a\neq b,c}(VV^{\top})_{a,b}(\beta^{-2})_{c,c}X_{a,c}X_{b,c}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\neq b,c}(VV^{\top})_{a,b}^{2}(\beta^{-2})_{c,c}^{2}\Var(X_{a,c}X_{b,c})

(86)

\displaystyle\asymp\rho_{n}\sum_{a,c}(VV^{\top})_{a,a}^{2}(\beta^{-2})_{c,c}^{2}+\rho_{n}^{2}\|VV^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\|\beta^{-2}\|_{F}^{2}.

We compare the above to the variance of the unweighted trace in the denominator. By a perfectly analogous variance expansion for
\tr(X\beta^{-2}X)=\sum_{a,c}(\beta^{-2})_{c,c}X_{a,c}^{2}
, one obtains

\displaystyle\Var\big(\tr(X\beta^{-2}X^{\top})\big)=\sum_{a,c}(\beta^{-2})_{c,c}^{2}\Var(X_{a,c}^{2})\asymp\rho_{n}\,n\,\|\beta^{-2}\|_{F}^{2}.

(87)

Combining the results from
Eqs.

85
,
86
and
87
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\Var\big(\tr(VV^{\top}X\beta^{-2}X^{\top})\big)}{\Var\big(\tr(X\beta^{-2}X^{\top})\big)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\rho_{n}\sum_{a}(VV^{\top})_{a,a}^{2}\|\beta^{-2}\|_{F}^{2}+\rho_{n}^{2}\|VV^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}\|\beta^{-2}\|_{F}^{2}}{\rho_{n}\,n\,\|\beta^{-2}\|_{F}^{2}}

(88)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{a}(VV^{\top})_{a,a}^{2}+\rho_{n}\|VV^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}}{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k(1+\rho_{n})}{n}\to 0,

where the last line follows from the fact that
VV^{\top}
is a projection matrix and
1
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

3

Proof.

Computing the mean
: The expectation of the second–order term can be written in a compact operator form. Using linearity of trace and expectation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\,2\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=2\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\tr(\beta^{-2}X\beta^{\perp}X)\bigr]=2\,\tr\bigl[\beta^{-2}\,\mathbb{E}(X\beta^{\perp}X)\bigr].

(89)

To evaluate
\mathbb{E}(X\beta^{\perp}X)
we use the independence and centering of the entries of
X
. Write
\Sigma:=P\circ(J_{n}-P)
for the entrywise variances of the Bernoulli observations. A straightforward bookkeeping of the contributing index configurations yields the decomposition of
\mathbb{E}(X\beta^{\perp}X)
into an off–diagonal Hadamard term and a diagonal correction:

\mathbb{E}(X\beta^{\perp}X)=\Sigma\circ\beta^{\perp}+\mathrm{Diag}(\Sigma\cdot d-\mathrm{diag}(\Sigma)\circ d),

where
d:=\mathrm{diag}(\beta^{\perp}).

Substituting this identity into
Eq.

89
produces the final mean expression

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\,2\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=2\,\tr\Bigl[\beta^{-2}\bigl(\Sigma\circ\beta^{\perp}+\mathrm{Diag}(\Sigma\cdot d-\mathrm{diag}(\Sigma)\circ d))\bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle=2\,\tr\Bigl[\beta^{-2}\bigl(\Sigma\circ\beta^{\perp}+\mathrm{Diag}(\Sigma\cdot d-\mathrm{diag}(\Sigma)\circ d))\bigr)\Bigr].

This identity is the deterministic mean contribution that appears in
Eq.

25
.

Computing the variance:
We now compute the variance of the leading second–order contribution.
Observe that since
\beta^{\perp}=I-VV^{\top}
, by the cyclic property of trace we have that

\displaystyle\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}=\tr(\beta^{-1}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1})=\tr(X\beta^{-2}X)-\tr(VV^{\top}X\beta^{-2}X).

From
Lemma

2
, it suffices to analyze the variance of

2\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}=2\,\tr(X\beta^{-2}X^{\top})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\,X_{i\cdot}^{\top}\beta^{-2}X_{i\cdot},

For a fixed
i
, define the quadratic form

\displaystyle Q_{i}:=2\,X_{i\cdot}^{\top}\beta^{-2}X_{i\cdot}=4\sum_{s<t}(\beta^{-2})_{st}\,{X_{is}}\,{X_{it}}+2\sum_{s=1}^{n}(\beta^{-2})_{ss}\,{X_{is}}^{2},

so that
2\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}Q_{i}
. The variance then decomposes as

\displaystyle\Var\left(2\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Var(Q_{i})+2\sum_{i<k}\Cov(Q_{i},Q_{k}).

(90)

For the diagonal terms, using independence and the standard fourth moment identity for centered Bernoulli variables, we obtain

\displaystyle\Var(Q_{i})=16\sum_{s<t}(\beta^{-2})_{st}^{2}\,\Sigma_{is}\,\Sigma_{it}+4\sum_{s=1}^{n}(\beta^{-2})_{ss}^{2}\,K_{is},

where

K_{is}:=\mathbb{E}({X_{is}}^{4})-\bigl(\mathbb{E}({X_{is}}^{2})\bigr)^{2}=2\,\Sigma_{is}(1-2P_{is})

is the fourth–moment correction term.

For the off–diagonal contributions, note that
Q_{i}
and
Q_{k}
share only the symmetric variable
x_{ik}=x_{ki}
. A direct calculation shows

\Cov(Q_{i},Q_{k})=4\,K_{ik}\,(\beta^{-2})_{ii}\,(\beta^{-2})_{kk}.

Combining the two components and rewriting the double sums from
Eq.

90
yields

\displaystyle\Var\left(2\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(16\sum_{s<t}(\beta^{-2})_{st}^{2}\,\Sigma_{is}\,\Sigma_{it}+4\sum_{s=1}^{n}(\beta^{-2})_{ss}^{2}\,K_{is}\right)+8\sum_{i<k}\,K_{ik}\,(\beta^{-2})_{ii}\,(\beta^{-2})_{kk}

\displaystyle=8\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{s\neq t}(\beta^{-2})_{st}^{2}\,\Sigma_{is}\,\Sigma_{it}\right)+\left(4\sum_{s=1}^{n}(\beta^{-2})_{ss}^{2}\,K_{is}+4\sum_{i\neq k}\,K_{ik}\,(\beta^{-2})_{ii}\,(\beta^{-2})_{kk}\right)

\displaystyle=8\big\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\beta^{-2}\circ\beta^{-2}),\,\Sigma^{2}\big\rangle+4\,\alpha^{\top}K\alpha.

This expression coincides with the variance term in
Eq.

26
.

Finally, we compute the asymptotic order of the variance obtained above.
From
1
,
3
and
4
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}(\beta^{-2})_{ij}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\beta^{-2}\|_{F}^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\beta^{-2})_{ii}^{2}

(91)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\tr(\beta^{-4})-\max_{i,j}(\beta^{-2})_{ij}\tr(\beta^{-2})

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}.

Similarly it can be shown that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}(\beta^{-2})_{ij}^{2}\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}

(92)

which combined with
Eq.

91
yields

\sum_{i\neq j}(\beta^{-2})_{ij}^{2}\asymp\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}.

(93)

Therefore it follows that

\displaystyle 8\big\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\beta^{-2}\circ\beta^{-2}),\,\Sigma^{2}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=8\sum_{i\neq j}(\beta^{-2})_{ij}^{2}(\Sigma^{2})_{ij}\asymp n\rho_{n}^{2}\cdot\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}\asymp\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\alpha^{\top}K\alpha

\displaystyle=\rho_{n}(\sum_{i}\alpha_{i})^{2}=\rho_{n}\tr(\beta^{-2})^{2}=\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{3}}.

Thus, it follows that

\Var\left(2\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\right)\asymp\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}.

Hence, by
Lemma

2
, we obtain

\Var\left(2\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\right)=8\big\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\beta^{-2}\circ\beta^{-2}),\,\Sigma^{2}\big\rangle+4\,\alpha^{\top}K\alpha+o\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

∎

Proof of
Lemma

4

Proof.

Suppose that

\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}=\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}+R.

By
Lemma

2
, we have

\Var(R)/\Var(T_{1}^{(S)})\to 0,

and consequently

(R-\mathbb{E}R)/\sqrt{\Var(T_{1}^{(S)})}\to 0

in probability. Hence the contribution of
R
is asymptotically negligible.
Therefore, in establishing the limiting distribution of

\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2},

it suffices to derive the asymptotic distribution of

\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}

after appropriate centering and scaling.
The desired result then follows by a direct application of Slutsky’s theorem.
For notational convenience set
U=\beta^{-1}
. Then

\|X\beta^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}=\tr(\beta^{-1}X^{2}\beta^{-1})=\tr(UX^{2}U),

and hence

n\tr(UX^{2}U)=n\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq i,r,q\leq n}{X_{ri}}{X_{qi}}U_{jr}U_{jq}.

Following the decomposition strategy employed in the proof of Lemma 2 in
26
, the right–hand side can be reorganized into the form used for a martingale central limit theorem (CLT). Concretely,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq i,r,q\leq n}n\,{X_{ri}}{X_{qi}}U_{jr}U_{jq}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq r<i\leq n}2{X_{ri}}\Bigl(n\,U_{jr}\sum_{1\leq q<r}{X_{iq}}U_{jq}+n\,U_{ji}\sum_{1\leq q<i}{X_{rq}}U_{jq}\Bigr)

(94)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq q<r\leq n}2n\,{X_{rr}}{X_{rq}}\,U_{jq}U_{jr}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{1\leq r<i\leq n}{X_{ri}}^{2}\bigl(n\,U_{jr}^{2}+n\,U_{ji}^{2}\bigr)+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{r=1}^{n}{X_{rr}}^{2}\,n\,U_{jr}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq r<i\leq n}2{X_{ri}}\Bigl(\sum_{1\leq q<r}{X_{iq}}\gamma_{rq}+\sum_{1\leq q<i}{X_{rq}}\gamma_{iq}\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq q<r\leq n}2\gamma_{rq}{X_{rr}}{X_{rq}}+\sum_{1\leq r<i\leq n}{X_{ri}}^{2}(\gamma_{rr}+\gamma_{ii})+\sum_{r=1}^{n}{X_{rr}}^{2}\gamma_{rr},

where we have set

\gamma_{r,q}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}n\,U_{jr}U_{jq}.
After centering we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}U_{j\cdot}^{\top}(X^{2}-\mathbb{E}X^{2})U_{j\cdot}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq r<i\leq n}2{X_{ri}}\Bigl(\sum_{1\leq q<r}{X_{iq}}\gamma_{rq}+\sum_{1\leq q<i}{X_{rq}}\gamma_{iq}\Bigr)+\sum_{1\leq q<r\leq n}2\gamma_{rq}{X_{rr}}{X_{rq}}

(95)

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq r<i\leq n}({X_{ri}}^{2}-\nu_{ri}^{2})(\gamma_{rr}+\gamma_{ii})+\sum_{r=1}^{n}({X_{rr}}^{2}-\sigma_{rr}^{2})\,\gamma_{rr}.

To set up a martingale difference array, we introduce the triangular filtration

\mathscr{F}_{t}:=\sigma(w_{1},\ldots,w_{t}),\qquad t=k+\tfrac{l(l-1)}{2},\quad 1\leq k\leq l\leq n,

so that
\mathscr{F}_{t}
records the entries
{X_{ij}}
revealed up to position
t
in the standard lexicographic ordering of the upper–triangular matrix. Under this filtration
Eq.

95
can be written as a sum of martingale differences. Each summand (the increment associated with the revealing of the entry
{X_{ri}}
) admits the decomposition

{X_{ri}}b_{ri}+({X_{ri}}^{2}-\nu_{ri}^{2})\,c_{ri},

with coefficients given by

\displaystyle b_{ri}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2\displaystyle\Bigl(\sum_{1\leq q<r}{X_{iq}}\gamma_{rq}+\sum_{1\leq q<i}{X_{rq}}\gamma_{iq}\Bigr),&r<i,\\[8.0pt]
\displaystyle 2\sum_{1\leq q<r}{X_{rq}}\gamma_{rq},&i=r,\end{cases}

(96)

\displaystyle c_{ri}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\gamma_{rr}+\gamma_{ii},&r<i,\\[6.0pt]
\gamma_{rr},&i=r.\end{cases}

The conditional variance (given the
\sigma
–field just prior to revealing
{X_{ri}}
) takes the explicit form

\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\nu_{ri}^{2}b_{ri}^{2}+2\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\theta_{ri}b_{ri}c_{ri}+\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\kappa_{ri}c_{ri}^{2},

(97)

where we adopt the notation

\nu_{ri}^{2}=\mathbb{E}[{X_{ri}}^{2}],\theta_{ri}=\mathbb{E}[{X_{ri}}^{3}],\kappa_{ri}=\mathbb{E}\bigl[({X_{ri}}^{2}-\nu_{ri}^{2})^{2}\bigr].

The verification of the martingale CLT requires control of the first four moments of the coefficients
b_{ri}
and the second and fourth moments of the conditional variance of
Eq.

97
. We proceed to record the necessary moment estimates. Using the definitions above, one checks that the leading order behavior of the coefficients is captured by the following asymptotic relations (the algebraic manipulations are elementary and follow from counting the contributing index configurations).

We calculate the necessary moment estimates. First, note that
\mathbb{E}[b_{ri}]=0
for all
r,i
. Also, under
1
,
3
and
4
, just like
Eq.

93
we can show that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\gamma_{ij}^{2}\asymp\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{4}},\quad\gamma_{ij}^{2}\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}

(98)

Thus,

\sum_{r\leq i}\mathbb{E}[b_{ri}^{2}]\asymp\sum_{r\leq i}\sum_{1\leq q<r}\gamma_{rq}^{2}\,\nu_{iq}^{2}+\sum_{r\leq i}\sum_{1\leq q<i}\gamma_{iq}^{2}\,\sigma_{rq}^{2}\asymp\frac{k}{n\rho_{n}^{2}}

where
\gamma_{rq}^{2}=(\sum_{r=1}^{n}nU_{jr}U_{jq})^{2}
.
Apart from that, we also have
\nu_{ri}^{2},\,\theta_{ri},\kappa_{ri}\asymp\rho_{n}
.

The fourth moment of
b_{ri}
admits the expansion

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[b_{ri}^{4}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{1\leq q<r}\gamma_{rq}^{4}\,\mathbb{E}[{X_{iq}}^{4}]+\sum_{1\leq q<i}\gamma_{iq}^{4}\,\mathbb{E}[{X_{rq}}^{4}]

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}\neq q_{2}\\
1\leq q_{1},q_{2}<r\end{subarray}}\gamma_{rq_{1}}^{2}\gamma_{rq_{2}}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}[{X_{iq_{1}}}^{2}]\,\mathbb{E}[{X_{iq_{2}}}^{2}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1}\neq q_{2}\\
1\leq q_{1},q_{2}<i\end{subarray}}\gamma_{iq_{1}}^{2}\gamma_{iq_{2}}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}[{X_{rq_{1}}}^{2}]\,\mathbb{E}[{X_{rq_{2}}}^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{6}}

Finally, for the
c_{ri}
terms we have the simpler bounds:

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\kappa_{ri}c_{ri}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}\left[n\sum_{i}\gamma_{ii}^{2}+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{ii}\right)^{2}\right]

(99)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{3}}

\displaystyle=o\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)

With these moment estimates in hand we evaluate the first two moments of the conditional variance of
Eq.

97
. Denote by
s_{V}^{2}
the expectation of the conditional variance and by
\kappa_{V}^{2}
its variance. A straightforward aggregation over indices yields the asymptotic relations

\displaystyle s_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\nu_{ri}^{2}b_{ri}^{2}+2\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\theta_{ri}b_{ri}c_{ri}+\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\kappa_{ri}c_{ri}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{k}{n\rho_{n}^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\kappa_{V}

\displaystyle=\Var\Big(\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\nu_{ri}^{2}b_{ri}^{2}+2\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\theta_{ri}b_{ri}c_{ri}+\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\kappa_{ri}c_{ri}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\Var(\nu_{ri}^{2}b_{ri}^{2})+\sum_{1\leq r\leq i\leq n}\Var(2\theta_{ri}b_{ri}c_{ri})\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}.

Combining the above estimates we obtain the Lyapunov–type ratio used to verify the martingale CLT:

\frac{s_{V}}{\kappa_{V}^{1/4}}=\frac{n\rho_{n}}{\sqrt{n}\rho_{n}}\gg 1.

Thus the Lyapunov type condition as used in Lemma 9.12 of
9
is satisfied.

Therefore the martingale central limit theorem applies and we conclude that the appropriately centered and scaled second–order term converges in distribution to a standard normal:

\frac{2\bigl\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\bigr\|_{F}^{2}-\,\mathbb{E}\left[\,2\bigl\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\bigr\|_{F}^{2}\right]}{\sqrt{\Var\left(2\bigl\|\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\bigr\|_{F}^{2}\right)}}\xrightarrow{D}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

(100)

∎

Proof of
Lemma

5

Proof.

We first decompose
\langle VV^{\top},\,S_{3}(X)\rangle
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\langle VV^{\top},S_{3}(X)\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\tr\big(\beta^{-1}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}X\beta^{-1}\big)\right)+\mathbb{E}\left(\tr\big(\beta^{-1}X\beta^{-1}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\big)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\left(\tr\big(\beta^{-2}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\big)\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\tr\big(\beta^{-1}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-2}\big)\right).

We compute the orders of

\mathbb{E}\left(\tr(\beta^{-1}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}X\beta^{-1})\right)

and

\mathbb{E}\left(\tr(\beta^{-2}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1})\right)
;
the remaining two terms follow identically. Note that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\left(\tr\big(\beta^{-1}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}X\beta^{-1}\big)\right)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(\beta^{-2})_{ji}\,(\beta^{\perp})_{ji}\,(\beta^{-1})_{ji}\,(X_{ij})^{3}\bigg|

(101)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right),

where the final line follows from

1
,
3
and
4
. Also, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\left(\tr\big(\beta^{-2}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}\big)\right)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(\beta^{-3})_{ji}\,(\beta^{\perp})_{ji}^{2}\,(X_{ij})^{3}\bigg|

(102)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

Combining
Eqs.

101
and
102
establishes the claim and completes the proof of
Lemma

5
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

6

Proof.

To evaluate the order of
\Var\!\big(\langle VV^{\top},S_{3}(X)\rangle\big)
, it suffices to show that

\frac{\Var\!\big(\langle VV^{\top},S_{3}(X)\rangle\big)}{\Var\!\left(\tr\!\big(\beta^{-3}X^{3}\big)\right)}\asymp 1

and calculate the order of
\Var\!\left(\tr\!\big(\beta^{-3}X^{3}\big)\right).

We proceed by comparing cyclically reduced representatives. Define

R_{1}:=\tr\!\big(\beta^{-3}X^{3}\big),\qquad R_{2}:=\tr\!\big(\beta^{-3}X(\beta^{\perp}X)^{2}\big).

We first establish that

\frac{\Var(R_{1})}{\Var(R_{2})}\to 1.

To this end, insert the decomposition
I=VV^{\top}+\beta^{\perp}
in each interior gap of
R_{1}
to obtain

\displaystyle R_{1}=\sum_{\sigma\in\{VV^{\top},\,\beta^{\perp}\}^{\,2}}\tr\big(\beta^{-3}XM_{\sigma,1}XM_{\sigma,2}X\big),

(103)

where each
M_{\sigma,r}\in\{VV^{\top},\beta^{\perp}\}
.
The unique summand for which every
M_{\sigma,r}=\beta^{\perp}
is exactly
R_{2}
; every other summand (henceforth a
remainder
) contains at least one factor
VV^{\top}
.
Since the total number of summands is finite, it suffices to prove that each remainder has variance
o(\Var(R_{2}))
; the claim then follows by finite summation.

Fix a remainder monomial

\mathcal{M}:=\tr\big(\beta^{-3}XM_{1}XM_{2}X\big).

Expanding the trace yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\atop j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}(\beta^{-3})_{j_{1},i_{1}}\,X_{i_{1},j_{2}}\,(M_{1})_{j_{2},i_{2}}\,X_{i_{2},j_{3}}\,(M_{2})_{j_{3},i_{3}}\,X_{i_{3},j_{1}}

(104)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\atop j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}w(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})\,X_{i_{1},j_{2}}\,X_{i_{2},j_{3}}\,X_{i_{3},j_{1}},

where

w(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}):=(\beta^{-3})_{j_{1},i_{1}}(M_{1})_{j_{2},i_{2}}(M_{2})_{j_{3},i_{3}}.

In order for

\Cov\big(w(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})\,X_{i_{1},j_{2}}\,X_{i_{2},j_{3}}\,X_{i_{3},j_{1}},w(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\,X_{k_{1},l_{2}}\,X_{k_{2},l_{3}}\,X_{k_{3},l_{1}}\big)\neq 0

to hold, the underlying edge configurations must coincide. Without loss of generality, the edge
(k_{2},l_{3})
must coincide with
(i_{2},j_{3})
, and the subgraph
\{(k_{1},l_{2}),(k_{3},l_{1})\}
must coincide with
\{(i_{1},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{1})\}
. Alternatively, the edge
(k_{1},l_{2})
is identified with
(i_{1},j_{2})
, or
(k_{3},l_{1})
with
(i_{3},j_{1})
, with the corresponding subgraph matching accordingly. Consequently, these constraints imply that the index tuples
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})
agree with
(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})
up to the natural symmetries of the configuration, a relationship we denote by
(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\sim(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})
. Thus, we obtain

\Var(\mathcal{M})\asymp\sum_{(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\sim(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})}w(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})w(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\,\rho_{n}^{3}.

Define the quantity

\Sigma(\mathcal{M}):=\sum_{(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\sim(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})}w(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})w(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})=\sum_{(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\sim(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})}\Big((\beta^{-3})_{j_{1},i_{1}}\,(M_{1})_{j_{1},i_{2}}\,(M_{2})_{j_{2},i_{3}}\Big)\Big((\beta^{-3})_{l_{1},k_{1}}\,(M_{1})_{l_{1},k_{2}}\,(M_{2})_{l_{2},k_{3}}\Big).

Invoking
3
and
1
, we find that

\Sigma(\mathcal{M})\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{8}\rho_{n}^{6}}\sum_{(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\sim(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})}\Big((M_{1})_{j_{1},i_{2}}\,(M_{2})_{j_{2},i_{3}}\Big)\Big((M_{1})_{l_{1},k_{2}}\,(M_{2})_{l_{2},k_{3}}\Big).

Consider the configuration where
M_{1}=VV^{\top}
and
M_{2}=\beta^{\perp}
. Evaluating
\Sigma(\mathcal{M})
requires summing over six structural indices:
i_{1},j_{1},i_{2},j_{2},i_{3},\text{ and }j_{3}
. However, because
M_{2}=\beta^{\perp}
, it enforces an index collapse (specifically,
j_{2}=i_{3}
). This structural constraint reduces the effective degrees of freedom in the summation from six to five, yielding a factor of
O(n^{5})
. Coupling this with the entrywise bound of
(k/n)^{2}
for
M_{1}
, guaranteed by the incoherence condition in
4
, we directly obtain the upper bound:

\Sigma(\mathcal{M})\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{8}\rho_{n}^{6}}\cdot n^{5}\cdot\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{2}\lesssim\frac{k^{4}}{n^{5}\rho_{n}^{6}}.

Similarly, if
M_{1}=M_{2}=VV^{\top}
, we have

\Sigma(\mathcal{M})\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{8}\rho_{n}^{6}}\cdot n^{6}\cdot\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)^{4}\lesssim\frac{k^{6}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{6}}.

Thus, it follows that whenever at least one of
M_{1}
or
M_{2}
equals
VV^{\top}
, the variance of the monomial satisfies

\displaystyle\Var(\mathcal{M})=o(\Var(R_{2})).

(105)

By the same covariance calculation over matching edge configurations, under
3
,
4
and
1
we have

\displaystyle\Var(R_{1})

\displaystyle=\Var(\tr(\beta^{-3}X^{3}))

(106)

\displaystyle\asymp n^{2}\rho_{n}^{3}\|\beta^{-3}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{3}}

Combining
Eqs.

103
and
106
with

Eq.

105
establishes the claim that

\displaystyle\frac{\Var(R_{1})}{\Var(R_{2})}=\frac{\Var(\sum_{\sigma\in\{VV^{\top},\,\beta^{\perp}\}^{\,2}}\tr\big(\beta^{-3}XM_{\sigma,1}XM_{\sigma,2}X\big))}{\Var(R_{2})}=\frac{\Var(R_{2})+o(\Var(R_{2}))}{\Var(R_{2})}\to 1.

(107)

In exactly the same manner, we can show that

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-t}X^{t}\beta^{-(3-t)}X^{3-t}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-t}X(\beta^{\perp}X)^{t-1}\beta^{-(3-t)}X(\beta^{\perp}X)^{3-t-1}\right)\right)}\to 1

for
t=1,2
and

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-1}X\beta^{-1}X\beta^{-1}X\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-1}X\beta^{-1}(\beta^{\perp}X)\beta^{-1}(\beta^{\perp}X)\right)\right)}\to 1.

To establish that the dominant variance contributions in

\langle VV^{\top},\,S_{3}(X)\rangle
arise from terms of the form

\tr\big(\beta^{-s_{1}}X\,\beta^{\perp}X\cdots\beta^{\perp}X\,\beta^{-s_{4}}\big),

it therefore suffices to show that for
t=1,2
,

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-t}X^{t}\,\beta^{-(3-t)}X^{3-t}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-3}X^{3}\right)\right)}\to 0,\qquad\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-1}X\beta^{-1}X\beta^{-1}X\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-3}X^{3}\right)\right)}\to 0.

The proof technique for both ratios is identical; hence we concentrate on the first.

Write the numerator trace as

R_{1,\mathrm{num}}:=\tr\big(\beta^{-t}X^{t}\,\beta^{-(3-t)}X^{3-t}\big).

Proceeding as in the preceding argument, we obtain

\displaystyle\Var(R_{1,\mathrm{num}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}^{3}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}\atop j_{1},j_{2}}\left[(\beta^{-t})_{i_{1},j_{1}}\,(\beta^{-(3-t)})_{i_{2},j_{2}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}^{3}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}\atop j_{1},j_{2}}\frac{k^{4}}{n^{10}\rho_{n}^{6}}\lesssim\frac{k^{4}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{3}}.

Using
Eqs.

105
and
106
we will therefore have

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-t}X^{t}\,\beta^{-(3-t)}X^{3-t}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{-3}X^{3}\right)\right)}=\frac{\frac{k^{4}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{3}}}{\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{3}}}\to 0,

as required. This establishes that the dominant contributions to

\Var\!\big(\langle VV^{\top},S_{3}(X)\rangle\big)

arise precisely from summands of the form

\tr\big(\beta^{-s_{1}}X\,\beta^{\perp}X\cdots\beta^{\perp}X\,\beta^{-s_{4}}\big)
.
In particular, we have shown that

\frac{\Var\!\big(\langle VV^{\top},S_{3}(X)\rangle\big)}{\Var\!\left(\tr\!\big(\beta^{-3}X^{3}\big)\right)}\asymp 1.

Combining this with the variance order in
Eq.

106
completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of Lemmas from
Appendix

B

In this section we prove the auxiliary lemmas required for the proof of
Theorem

1
in
Appendix

B
.

Proof of
Lemma

7

Proof.

Define

M(X)=\begin{pmatrix}{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&-{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\\
-{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\end{pmatrix},\qquad M(Y)=\begin{pmatrix}(Y^{(1)})^{\top}(Y^{(1)})&-(Y^{(1)})^{\top}(Y^{(2)})\\
-(Y^{(2)})^{\top}(Y^{(1)})&(Y^{(2)})^{\top}(Y^{(2)})\end{pmatrix}.

It therefore suffices to analyze

\displaystyle\frac{\Var\left(2\,\tr\big(U(M(Y)-M(X))U^{\top}\big)\right)}{\Var\left(2\,\tr\big(UM(X)U^{\top}\big)\right)}.

Observe that

\displaystyle M(X)-M(Y)=\begin{pmatrix}{X^{(1)}}^{\top}W_{11}{X^{(1)}}&{X^{(1)}}^{\top}W_{12}{X^{(2)}}\\
{X^{(2)}}^{\top}W_{21}{X^{(1)}}&{X^{(2)}}^{\top}W_{22}{X^{(2)}}\end{pmatrix}.

(108)

Denote the
(i,j)
-th block of
M(X)-M(Y)
by
{X^{(i)}}^{\top}W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}
, where

\displaystyle W_{11}

\displaystyle={V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top},

(109)

\displaystyle W_{12}=W_{21}

\displaystyle=-({V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}+{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}-{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}),

\displaystyle W_{22}

\displaystyle={V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}.

Then,

\displaystyle\Var\left(\tr\big(U(M(Y)-M(X))U^{\top}\big)\right)=\Var\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\tr\left(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top}\right)\right).

Hence, to demonstrate that

\Var\big(\tr(U(M(Y)-M(X))U^{\top})\big)

is asymptotically negligible compared to

\Var\big(\tr(UM(X)U^{\top})\big)
,
it suffices to consider any fixed pair
(i,j)
and establish that

\displaystyle\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left({X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top}\right)\right)}\to 0.

(110)

By conditioning on
{X^{(i)}}
(recall
{X^{(j)}}
is independent of
{X^{(i)}}
and has mean zero) we have

\displaystyle\Var\big(\tr\big(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top}\big)\big)=\mathbb{E}\Big[\Var\big(\tr\big(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top}\big)\bigm|{X^{(i)}}\big)\Big].

Writing the trace in entrywise form yields

\tr\big(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top}\big)=\sum_{b,c}({X^{(j)}})_{b,c}s_{bc}\big(({X^{(i)}})\big),

where, for each pair
(b,c)
,

s_{bc}\big(({X^{(i)}})\big):=\sum_{a,d}W_{ab}\,(\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1})_{cd}\,({X^{(i)}})_{a,d}.

By independence (up to symmetry), there exist positive constants
C_{1},C_{2}
(independent of
n
) such that for every fixed realization of
{X^{(i)}}
,

C_{1}\sum_{b,c}\Var\big(({X^{(j)}})_{b,c}\big)\,s_{bc}\big(({X^{(i)}})\big)^{2}\leq\Var\big(\tr(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top})\bigm|{X^{(i)}}\big)\leq C_{2}\sum_{b,c}\Var\big(({X^{(j)}})_{b,c}\big)\,s_{bc}\big(({X^{(i)}})\big)^{2}.

(111)

Taking expectation with respect to
{X^{(i)}}
, and using
\Var(({X^{(j)}})_{b,c})\asymp\rho_{n}
uniformly in
(b,c)
, we obtain

\Var\big(\tr(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top})\big)\asymp\rho_{n}\sum_{b,c}\mathbb{E}\big[s_{bc}\big(({X^{(i)}})\big)^{2}\big].

(112)

We now expand
\mathbb{E}\big[s_{bc}(({X^{(i)}}))^{2}\big]
. Using independence (up to symmetry) of the entries of
{X^{(i)}}
and the uniform variance bound
\Var(({X^{(i)}})_{a,d})\asymp\rho_{n}
,
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[s_{bc}\big(({X^{(i)}})\big)^{2}\big]=\sum_{a,d}\bigg(W_{ab}^{2}\,(\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1})_{cd}^{2}+W_{db}^{2}\,(\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1})_{ca}^{2}\bigg)\,\mathbb{E}\big[({X^{(i)}})_{a,d}^{2}\big],

and hence

\mathbb{E}\big[s_{bc}\big(({X^{(i)}})\big)^{2}\big]\asymp\rho_{n}\sum_{a,d}\bigg(W_{ab}^{2}\,(\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1})_{cd}^{2}+W_{db}^{2}\,(\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1})_{ca}^{2}\bigg).

(113)

Summing
Eq.

113
over
b,c
and reordering the sums yields

\displaystyle\sum_{b,c}\mathbb{E}\big[s_{bc}\big(({X^{(i)}})\big)^{2}\big]\asymp\rho_{n}\sum_{a,b}\sum_{c,d}W_{ab}^{2}\,(\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1})_{cd}^{2}\asymp\rho_{n}\,\|W_{ij}\|_{F}^{2}\,\|\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}.

Combining this with
Eq.

112
shows that

\Var\big(\tr(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top})\big)\asymp\rho_{n}^{2}\,\|W_{ij}\|_{F}^{2}\,\|\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}.

(114)

We compare the above to the variance of the unweighted trace. By an identical conditioning argument, now with the coefficient

r_{mp}\big(({X^{(i)}})\big):=\sum_{q}(\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1})_{pq}\,({X^{(i)}})_{m,q},

appearing in the expansion
\tr({X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top})=\sum_{m,p}({X^{(j)}})_{m,p}\,r_{mp}(({X^{(i)}}))
, one obtains

\Var\big(\tr({X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top})\big)\asymp\rho_{n}^{2}\,n\,\|\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}.

(115)

Dividing
Eq.

114
by
Eq.

115
yields

\frac{\Var\big(\tr(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top})\big)}{\Var\big(\tr({X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top})\big)}\asymp\frac{\|W_{ij}\|_{F}^{2}}{n}.

Recalling the definition of
W_{ij}
in
Eq.

109
, we note that

\|{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}=k
and
\|{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}=k
, which follow immediately from the orthonormality of the columns of
{V^{(1)}}
and
{V^{(2)}}
.
Moreover,

\|{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}+{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}-{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}=\|{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2}+\|{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}-{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\|_{F}^{2},

which is bounded above by
2k
and below by
k
. Consequently,

\|W_{ij}\|_{F}^{2}\asymp k
.
It follows that

\frac{\Var\!\big(\tr(W_{ij}{X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top})\big)}{\Var\!\big(\tr({X^{(j)}}\beta_{j}^{-1}\beta_{i}^{-1}{X^{(i)}}^{\top})\big)}\asymp\frac{k}{n}\longrightarrow 0,

which completes the argument for
Eq.

110
, thereby proving the lemma.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

8

Proof of
Lemma

8
.

Step 1: computing the mean.
For independent, mean-zero matrices
{X^{(1)}}
and
{X^{(2)}}
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl\|\beta^{\perp}({X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}-{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1})\bigr\|_{F}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\,\tr\bigl(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-2}{X^{(1)}}\bigr)+\mathbb{E}\,\tr\bigl(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-2}{X^{(2)}}\bigr),

since the cross terms vanish by independence. Using the one-sample expectation result from
Lemma

3
, for each
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,({X^{(i)}}\beta_{i}^{\perp}{X^{(i)}})={\Sigma^{(i)}}\circ\beta_{i}^{\perp}+\Diag\bigl({\Sigma^{(i)}}\cdot d_{i}-\mathrm{diag}\left({\Sigma^{(i)}}\right)\circ d_{i}\bigr),

where

d_{i}=\mathrm{diag}(\beta_{i}^{\perp})
.
Consequently, the leading mean term satisfies

\displaystyle\mu_{2}=2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tr\left[\beta_{i}^{-2}\Bigl({\Sigma^{(i)}}\circ\beta_{i}^{\perp}+\Diag\bigl({\Sigma^{(i)}}\cdot d_{i}-\mathrm{diag}\left({\Sigma^{(i)}}\right)\circ d_{i}\bigr)\Bigr)\right].

Step 2: Computing the variance
.
According to
Lemma

7
, it suffices to compute

\Var\bigg(2\,\tr\left(U^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&-{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\\[4.0pt]
-{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\end{pmatrix}U\right)\bigg).

Let

\displaystyle Q_{i}={(X^{(1)})_{i.}}^{\top}\beta_{1}^{-2}{(X^{(1)})_{i.}}+{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}^{\top}\beta_{2}^{-2}{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}-{(X^{(1)})_{i.}}^{\top}\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}-{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}^{\top}\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-1}{(X^{(1)})_{i.}},

and define

\widetilde{T}_{2}^{(S)}=2\sum_{i=1}^{n}Q_{i}.

Then

\displaystyle\Var(\widetilde{T}_{2}^{(S)})=4\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Var(Q_{i})+8\sum_{1\leq i<k\leq n}\Cov(Q_{i},Q_{k}).

(116)

First, we will calculate
\Var(Q_{i}).
For each sample
r\in\{1,2\}
, the variance of the individual quadratic form
Q_{i}^{(r)}={(X^{(r)})_{i.}}^{\top}\beta_{r}^{-2}{(X^{(r)})_{i.}}

follows directly from the one-sample calculation (see
Section

E.3
)

\displaystyle\Var(Q_{i}^{(r)})=4\sum_{s<t}(\beta_{r}^{-2})_{st}^{2}\,\Sigma_{is}^{(r)}\Sigma_{it}^{(r)}+\sum_{s=1}^{n}(\beta_{r}^{-2})_{ss}^{2}\,K_{is}^{(r)},

(117)

where
\Sigma_{ij}^{(r)}=\Var({(X^{(r)})_{ij}})
and
K_{ij}^{(r)}=\mathbb{E}\left({(X^{(r)})_{ij}}^{4}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left({(X^{(r)})_{ij}}^{2}\right)^{2}
. Since the vectors
{(X^{(r)})_{i.}}
are centered, it follows immediately that

{(X^{(r_{1})})_{i.}}^{\top}\,\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\,{(X^{(r_{2})})_{i.}}
and

{(X^{(r_{1})})_{i.}}^{\top}\,\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\,{(X^{(r_{1})})_{i.}}

are independent whenever
(r_{1},r_{2})=(1,2)
.
Hence, for the purpose of evaluating
\Var(Q_{i})
, it suffices to compute

\Var\left({(X^{(1)})_{i.}}^{\top}\,\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\,{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}+{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}^{\top}\,\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-1}\,{(X^{(1)})_{i.}}\right).

A direct calculation shows that

\ \Var\left({(X^{(1)})_{i.}}^{\top}\,\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\,{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}\right)=\sum_{s,t}M_{s,t}^{2}\Sigma_{is}^{(1)}\Sigma_{is}^{(2)},

where
M:=\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}.

Therefore, the total variance contribution from the bilinear cross–sample term equals

\displaystyle\Var\left({(X^{(1)})_{i.}}^{\top}\,\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\,{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}+{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}^{\top}\,\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-1}\,{(X^{(1)})_{i.}}\right)=\sum_{s,t}G_{st}^{2}\,\Sigma_{is}^{(1)}\Sigma_{it}^{(2)},

(118)

where
G:=M+M^{\top},

which completes the calculation.

We now compute the covariance terms.
Decompose
Q_{i}=Q_{i}^{(1)}+Q_{i}^{(2)}-Q_{i}^{(1,2)}
such that

Q_{i}^{(1)}={(X^{(1)})_{i.}}^{\top}\beta_{1}^{-2}{(X^{(1)})_{i.}},Q_{i}^{(2)}={(X^{(2)})_{i.}}^{\top}\beta_{2}^{-2}{(X^{(2)})_{i.}},Q_{i}^{(1,2)}={(X^{(1)})_{i.}}^{\top}M{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}+{(X^{(2)})_{i.}}^{\top}M^{\top}{(X^{(1)})_{i.}}.

By bilinearity of covariance,

\displaystyle\Cov(Q_{i},Q_{k})

\displaystyle=\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1)},Q_{k}^{(1)})+\Cov(Q_{i}^{(2)},Q_{k}^{(2)})-\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1)},Q_{k}^{(1,2)})-\Cov(Q_{i}^{(2)},Q_{k}^{(1,2)})

\displaystyle-\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1,2)},Q_{k}^{(1)})-\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1,2)},Q_{k}^{(2)})+\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1,2)},Q_{k}^{(1,2)}).

Observe that
\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1)},Q_{k}^{(1,2)})=0
because
Q_{k}^{(1,2)}
is a linear combination of products
{X^{(1)}_{ks}}{X^{(2)}_{kt}}
,
and by independence of
{X^{(1)}}
and
{X^{(2)}}
together with
\mathbb{E}[{X^{(2)}_{kt}}]=0
, every term in
\mathbb{E}[Q_{i}^{(1)}Q_{k}^{(1,2)}]
vanishes.
The same reasoning yields
\Cov(Q_{i}^{(2)},Q_{k}^{(1,2)})=\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1,2)},Q_{i}^{(1)})=\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1,2)},Q_{k}^{(2)})=0
,
and independence across samples gives
\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1)},Q_{k}^{(2)})=\Cov(Q_{i}^{(2)},Q_{k}^{(1)})=0
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\Cov(Q_{i},Q_{k})=\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1)},Q_{k}^{(1)})+\Cov(Q_{i}^{(2)},Q_{k}^{(2)})+\Cov(Q_{i}^{(12)},Q_{k}^{(12)}).

Expanding

Q_{i}^{(1)}=\sum_{s,t}(\beta_{1}^{-2})_{st}\,{X^{(1)}_{is}}{X^{(1)}_{it}},

and

Q_{k}^{(1)}=\sum_{u,v}(\beta_{1}^{-2})_{uv}\,{X^{(1)}_{ku}}{X^{(1)}_{kv}},

the only shared random variable for
i\neq k
is
{X^{(1)}_{ik}}={X^{(1)}_{ki}}
.
Thus all cross-terms vanish except the product involving
\left({X^{(1)}_{ik}}\right)^{2}
,
leading to

\displaystyle\Cov(Q_{i}^{(1)},Q_{k}^{(1)})=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left({X^{(1)}_{ik}}\right)^{4}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\left({X^{(1)}_{ik}}\right)^{2}\right]^{2}\right)\,\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}\right)_{ii}\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}\right)_{kk}=K_{ik}^{(1)}(\beta_{1}^{-2})_{ii}(\beta_{1}^{-2})_{kk}.

Analogously,

\Cov(Q_{i}^{(2)},Q_{k}^{(2)})=K_{ik}^{(2)}(\beta_{2}^{-2})_{ii}(\beta_{2}^{-2})_{kk}.

Writing

Q_{i}^{(12)}=\sum_{s,t}G_{st}\,{X^{(1)}_{is}}{X^{(2)}_{it}},

and exploiting the independence of
{X^{(1)}}
and
{X^{(2)}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Q_{i}^{(12)}Q_{k}^{(12)}]=\sum_{s,t,u,v}G_{st}G_{uv}\,\mathbb{E}[{X^{(1)}_{is}}{X^{(1)}_{ku}}]\,\mathbb{E}[{X^{(2)}_{it}}{X^{(2)}_{kv}}].

For
i\neq k
, the only index combination contributing a nonzero expectation is
(s,u)=(k,i)
and
(t,v)=(i,k)
,
yielding

\Cov(Q_{i}^{(12)},Q_{k}^{(12)})=G_{ik}^{2}\,\Sigma_{ik}^{(1)}\Sigma_{ik}^{(2)}.
Combining all nonzero contributions, for
i\neq k
,

\displaystyle\Cov(Q_{i},Q_{k})=K_{ik}^{(1)}(\beta_{1}^{-2})_{ii}(\beta_{1}^{-2})_{kk}+K_{ik}^{(2)}(\beta_{2}^{-2})_{ii}(\beta_{2}^{-2})_{kk}+G_{ik}^{2}\,\Sigma_{ik}^{(1)}\Sigma_{ik}^{(2)}.

(119)

Let
\alpha_{r}=\mathrm{diag}(\beta_{r}^{-2})
.
Summing the within and between index contributions from
Eqs.

116
,
117
,
118
and
119
, and including the cross-term variance from the bilinear component, yields

\displaystyle\Var(\widetilde{T}_{2}^{(S)})

\displaystyle=8\sum_{r=1}^{2}\Big\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\beta_{r}^{-2}\circ\beta_{r}^{-2}),\,(\Sigma^{(r)})^{2}\Big\rangle+4\sum_{r=1}^{2}\alpha_{r}^{\top}K^{(r)}\alpha_{r}

\displaystyle+4\Big\langle G\circ G,{\Sigma^{(1)}}^{\top}{\Sigma^{(2)}}+(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ({\Sigma^{(1)}}\circ{\Sigma^{(2)}})\Big\rangle,

where
G=\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}+\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-1}
. From
1
,
3
and
4
, we have

K^{(r)}_{ij}\asymp\rho_{n},
and
\Sigma^{(r)}_{ij}\asymp\rho_{n}
for
r=1,2.
Plugging these values in our expression using the identity
Eq.

93
just like in the last paragraph of
Section

E.3
, it follows that
\Var(\widetilde{T}_{2}^{(S)})\asymp\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}
. Hence by
Lemma

7
, we have

\Var\bigl(T_{2}^{(S)}\bigr)=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\Bigl(8\,\bigl\langle(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ(\beta_{i}^{-2}\circ\beta_{i}^{-2}),({\Sigma^{(i)}})^{2}\bigr\rangle+4\,\alpha_{i}^{\top}K^{(i)}\alpha_{i}\Bigr)+4\,\bigl\langle G\circ G,{\Sigma^{(1)}}^{\top}{\Sigma^{(2)}}+(J_{n}-I_{n})\circ({\Sigma^{(1)}}\circ{\Sigma^{(2)}})\bigr\rangle+o\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

∎

Proof of
Lemma

9

Proof of
Lemma

9
.

We will prove this using a very similar approach to the proof of
Lemma

4
. Suppose that

\underbrace{2\,\tr\left(U^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}(Y^{(1)})^{\top}(Y^{(1)})&-(Y^{(1)})^{\top}(Y^{(2)})\\[4.0pt]
-(Y^{(2)})^{\top}(Y^{(1)})&(Y^{(2)})^{\top}(Y^{(2)})\end{pmatrix}U\right)}_{T_{2}^{(S)}}=\underbrace{2\,\tr\left(U^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&-{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\\[4.0pt]
-{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\end{pmatrix}U\right)}_{\widetilde{T}_{2}^{(S)}}+R,

with the same notation as in
Lemma

8
. By
Lemma

2
, we have

\Var(R)/\Var(T_{2}^{(S)})\to 0,

and consequently

(R-\mathbb{E}(R))/\sqrt{\Var(T_{2}^{(S)})}\to 0

in probability. Hence the contribution of
R
is asymptotically negligible.
Therefore, in establishing the limiting distribution of

T_{2}^{(S)},

it suffices to derive the asymptotic distribution of

\widetilde{T}_{2}^{(S)}

after appropriate centering and scaling.
The desired result then follows by a direct application of Slutsky’s theorem. We expand the leading-order term via

\displaystyle n\mathrm{tr}\bigg(U^{\top}
