\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&-{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\\
-{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\end{pmatrix}U\bigg)

(120)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}n\Big[U_{kj}U_{lj}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{1})_{il}+U_{n+k,j}U_{n+l,j}(x_{2})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}-2\,U_{kj}U_{n+l,j}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}\Big].

Regrouping terms using
\gamma_{a,b}:=n\sum_{j=1}^{n}U_{aj}U_{bj}
and rearranging, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}n\mathrm{tr}\bigg(U^{\top}&\begin{pmatrix}{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&-{X^{(1)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\\
-{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(1)}}&{X^{(2)}}^{\top}{X^{(2)}}\end{pmatrix}U\bigg)\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\Big[\gamma_{k,l}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{1})_{il}+\gamma_{n+k,n+l}(x_{2})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}-2\,\gamma_{k,n+l}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}\Big]\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,l=1\\
k>l\end{subarray}}^{n}\Big[2\gamma_{k,l}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{1})_{il}+2\gamma_{n+k,n+l}(x_{2})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}-2\,\gamma_{k,n+l}(x_{1})_{il}(x_{2})_{ik}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+k}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}\Big]\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\Big[\gamma_{k,k}(x_{1})_{ik}^{2}+\gamma_{n+k,n+k}(x_{2})_{ik}^{2}-2\,\gamma_{k,n+k}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{ik}\Big]\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,l=1\\
k>l,\,k<i\end{subarray}}^{n}\Big[2\gamma_{k,l}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{1})_{il}+2\gamma_{n+k,n+l}(x_{2})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}-2\,\gamma_{k,n+l}(x_{1})_{il}(x_{2})_{ik}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+k}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}\Big]\\
&\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,k,l=1\\
l<i,\,k<i\end{subarray}}^{n}\Big[2\gamma_{i,l}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{1})_{kl}+2\gamma_{n+i,n+l}(x_{2})_{ik}(x_{2})_{kl}-2\,\gamma_{i,n+l}(x_{1})_{kl}(x_{2})_{ik}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+i}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{lk}\Big]\\
&\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,l=1\\
l<k\end{subarray}}^{n}\Big[2\gamma_{k,l}(x_{1})_{kk}(x_{1})_{kl}+2\gamma_{n+k,n+l}(x_{2})_{kk}(x_{2})_{kl}-2\,\gamma_{k,n+l}(x_{1})_{kl}(x_{2})_{kk}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+k}(x_{1})_{kk}(x_{2})_{lk}\Big]\\
&\quad+\sum_{i,k=1}^{n}\Big[\gamma_{k,k}(x_{1})_{ik}^{2}+\gamma_{n+k,n+k}(x_{2})_{ik}^{2}-2\gamma_{k,n+k}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{ik}\Big]\\
&=\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}\Bigg[\sum_{1\leq l<k\leq n}\big(2\gamma_{k,l}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{1})_{il}+2\gamma_{n+k,n+l}(x_{2})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}-2\,\gamma_{k,n+l}(x_{1})_{il}(x_{2})_{ik}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+k}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}\big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{1\leq l<i\leq n}\big(2\gamma_{i,l}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{1})_{kl}+2\gamma_{n+i,n+l}(x_{2})_{ik}(x_{2})_{kl}-2\,\gamma_{i,n+l}(x_{1})_{kl}(x_{2})_{ik}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+i}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{lk}\big)\Bigg]\\
&\quad+\sum_{1\leq l<k\leq n}\big(2\gamma_{k,l}(x_{1})_{kk}(x_{1})_{kl}+2\gamma_{n+k,n+l}(x_{2})_{kk}(x_{2})_{kl}-2\,\gamma_{k,n+l}(x_{1})_{kl}(x_{2})_{kk}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+k}(x_{1})_{kk}(x_{2})_{lk}\big)\\
&\quad+\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}\big[(\gamma_{k,k}+\gamma_{i,i})(x_{1})_{ik}^{2}+(\gamma_{n+k,n+k}+\gamma_{n+i,n+i})(x_{2})_{ik}^{2}-2(\gamma_{k,n+k}+\gamma_{i,n+i})(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{ik}\big]\\
&\quad+\sum_{1\leq k\leq n}\big[\gamma_{k,k}(x_{1})_{kk}^{2}+\gamma_{n+k,n+k}(x_{2})_{kk}^{2}-2\gamma_{n+k,k}(x_{1})_{kk}(x_{2})_{kk}\big].\end{split}

Now consider the centered version of the quadratic form above. The centering ensures that each term has mean zero, which is essential for the martingale difference structure that follows. Explicitly, we write

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}\Bigg[\sum_{1\leq l<k\leq n}\big(2\gamma_{k,l}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{1})_{il}+2\gamma_{n+k,n+l}(x_{2})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}-2\,\gamma_{k,n+l}(x_{1})_{il}(x_{2})_{ik}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+k}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{il}\big)

(121)

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq l<i\leq n}\big(2\gamma_{i,l}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{1})_{kl}+2\gamma_{n+i,n+l}(x_{2})_{ik}(x_{2})_{kl}-2\,\gamma_{i,n+l}(x_{1})_{kl}(x_{2})_{ik}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+i}(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{lk}\big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq l<k\leq n}\big(2\gamma_{k,l}(x_{1})_{kk}(x_{1})_{kl}+2\gamma_{n+k,n+l}(x_{2})_{kk}(x_{2})_{kl}-2\,\gamma_{k,n+l}(x_{1})_{kl}(x_{2})_{kk}-2\,\gamma_{l,n+k}(x_{1})_{kk}(x_{2})_{lk}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}\big[(\gamma_{k,k}+\gamma_{i,i})\big((x_{1})_{ik}^{2}-(\sigma_{1})_{i,k}^{2}\big)+(\gamma_{n+k,n+k}+\gamma_{n+i,n+i})\big((x_{2})_{ik}^{2}-(\sigma_{2})_{i,k}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle-2(\gamma_{k,n+k}+\gamma_{i,n+i})(x_{1})_{ik}(x_{2})_{ik}\big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{1\leq k\leq n}\big[\gamma_{k,k}\big((x_{1})_{kk}^{2}-(\sigma_{1})_{k,k}^{2}\big)+\gamma_{n+k,n+k}\big((x_{2})_{kk}^{2}-(\sigma_{2})_{k,k}^{2}\big)-2\gamma_{n+k,k}(x_{1})_{kk}(x_{2})_{kk}\big],

where
\mathbb{E}\,(x_{i})_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{2}=(\sigma_{i})_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{2}
.
To employ the martingale central limit theorem, we introduce a filtration that orders the terms appropriately.
Define

\mathscr{F}_{t}:=\sigma({w}_{1},\ldots,{w}_{t}),\;\text{where }t=k+\frac{l(l-1)}{2},\;1\leq k\leq l\leq n,

so that

\mathscr{F}_{t}=\sigma\big((x_{m})_{ij}:1\leq i\leq j<l\ \text{or}\ 1\leq k\leq j<l,\ m=1,2\big).

Under this ordering, each summand in
Eq.

121
can be expressed as a martingale difference with respect to
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}
. Indeed, for
1\leq k\leq i\leq n
and
t=k+\frac{i(i-1)}{2}
,

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[(x_{1})_{i,k}(b_{1})_{i,k}+(x_{2})_{i,k}(b_{2})_{i,k}+\big((x_{1})^{2}_{i,k}-(\sigma_{1})^{2}_{i,k}\big)(c_{1})_{i,k}

(122)

\displaystyle+\big((x_{2})^{2}_{i,k}-(\sigma_{2})^{2}_{i,k}\big)(c_{2})_{i,k}+(x_{1})_{i,k}(x_{2})_{i,k}(c_{3})_{i,k}\ \big|\ \mathscr{F}_{t-1}\Big]=0,

where

\displaystyle(b_{1})_{i,k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq l<k}\big(2\gamma_{k,l}(x_{1})_{i,l}-2\gamma_{l,n+k}(x_{2})_{i,l}\big)+\sum_{1\leq l<i}\big(2\gamma_{i,l}(x_{1})_{k,l}-2\gamma_{l,n+i}(x_{2})_{l,k}\big),

\displaystyle(b_{2})_{i,k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq l<k}\big(2\gamma_{n+k,n+l}(x_{2})_{i,l}-2\gamma_{k,n+l}(x_{1})_{i,l}\big)+\sum_{1\leq l<i}\big(2\gamma_{n+i,n+l}(x_{2})_{k,l}-2\gamma_{i,n+l}(x_{1})_{k,l}\big),

\displaystyle(c_{1})_{i,k}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{k,k}+\gamma_{i,i},

\displaystyle(c_{2})_{i,k}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{n+k,n+k}+\gamma_{n+i,n+i},

\displaystyle(c_{3})_{i,k}

\displaystyle=-2\gamma_{k,n+k}-2\gamma_{i,n+i}.

From
Eq.

122
, the conditional variance of the sum in
Eq.

121
with respect to
\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}
denoted by
\eta
is given by:

\displaystyle\eta=\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}

\displaystyle\Big[(b_{1})^{2}_{i,k}(\sigma_{1})^{2}_{i,k}+(b_{2})^{2}_{i,k}(\sigma_{2})^{2}_{i,k}+(c_{1})^{2}_{i,k}(\kappa_{1})_{i,k}+(c_{2})^{2}_{i,k}(\kappa_{2})_{i,k}

(123)

\displaystyle+(c_{3})^{2}_{i,k}(\sigma_{1})^{2}_{i,k}(\sigma_{2})^{2}_{i,k}+(c_{1})_{i,k}(b_{1})_{i,k}(\theta_{1})_{i,k}+(c_{2})_{i,k}(b_{2})_{i,k}(\theta_{2})_{i,k}\Big],

where
(\kappa_{m})_{i,k}=\mathbb{E}\big[(x_{m})^{2}_{i,k}-(\sigma_{m})^{2}_{i,k}\big]^{2}
and
(\theta_{m})_{i,k}=\mathbb{E}\big[(x_{m})_{i,k}\big((x_{m})^{2}_{i,k}-(\sigma_{m})^{2}_{i,k}\big)\big]
.

This explicit martingale difference decomposition, together with the variance representation in
Eq.

123
, provides the necessary framework to invoke the martingale central limit theorem.
From the definition
U=\begin{pmatrix}\beta_{1}^{-1}&\beta_{2}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}
. Like
Eq.

93
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j}\gamma_{ij}^{2}\asymp\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{4}},\quad\gamma_{ij}^{2}\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}.

(124)

We first compute the order of
\mathbb{E}(\eta)
. From the definitions of
b_{i}
and
c_{i}
, it is straightforward to verify that

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}(\sigma_{1})_{ik}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[(b_{1})_{ik}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\gtrsim\rho_{n}^{2}\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}\left(\sum_{1\leq l<k}\gamma_{k,l}^{2}+\sum_{1\leq l<i}\gamma_{i,l}^{2}\right)

(125)

\displaystyle=\rho_{n}^{2}\left[\sum_{1\leq l<k\leq n}(n-k)\gamma_{k,l}^{2}+\sum_{1\leq l<i\leq n}(i-1)\gamma_{i,l}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=(n-1)\rho_{n}^{2}\sum_{1\leq l<k\leq n}\gamma_{k,l}^{2}

\displaystyle\asymp n\rho_{n}^{2}\cdot\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{4}}=\frac{k}{n\rho_{n}^{2}}.

Similarly we have

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}(\sigma_{2})_{ik}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[(b_{2})_{ik}^{2}\right]\gtrsim\frac{k}{n\rho_{n}^{2}}.

(126)

The other side of the bound follows trivially from
4
yielding

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}(\sigma_{m})_{ik}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[(b_{m})_{ik}^{2}\right]\asymp\frac{k}{n\rho_{n}^{2}}\quad\text{for}\quad m=1,2

(127)

Similar to
Eq.

99
, we also have the bounds

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}(\kappa_{1})_{i,k}(c_{1})^{2}_{i,k}\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{3}}=o\left(\frac{k}{n\rho_{n}^{2}}\right),\quad\sum_{1\leq k<i\leq n}(\sigma_{1})^{2}_{i,k}(\sigma_{2})^{2}_{i,k}(c_{3})^{2}_{i,k}\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{3}}=o\left(\frac{k}{n\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)

(128)

In addition, we have the bounds

(\kappa_{m})_{i,k}\asymp\rho_{n},\;(\theta_{m})_{i,k}\asymp\rho_{n},\;(\sigma_{m})^{2}_{i,k}\asymp\rho_{n}.

Combining these estimates, we find

\mathbb{E}(\eta)\asymp\frac{k}{n\rho_{n}^{2}}.

Next, we determine the order of
\Var(\eta)
. Since the terms
(c_{m})_{i,k}
are deterministic, they do not contribute directly to the variance; only the stochastic terms involving
(b_{m})_{i,k}
enter into
\Var(\eta)
. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[(b_{m})_{i,k}^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}\max_{1\leq a\leq 2n}\sum_{b=1}^{2n}\gamma_{a,b}^{4}+\rho_{n}^{2}\left(\max_{1\leq a\leq 2n}\sum_{b=1}^{2n}\gamma_{a,b}^{2}\right)^{2}

(129)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}\left(\max_{a,b}\gamma_{a,b}^{2}\right)\left(\max_{a}\sum_{b}\gamma_{a,b}^{2}\right)+\rho_{n}^{2}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{4}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{6}}.

and thus,

\displaystyle\Var\big((b_{m})^{2}_{i,k}(\sigma_{m})^{2}_{i,k}\big)\lesssim(\sigma_{m})^{4}_{i,k}\mathbb{E}((b_{m})^{4}_{i,k})\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{4}};

\displaystyle\Var\big((c_{m})_{i,k}(b_{m})_{i,k}(\theta_{m})_{i,k}\big)\lesssim\frac{k^{3}}{n^{7}\rho_{n}^{5}}.

Aggregating these contributions, we conclude that

\Var(\eta)\asymp\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}.

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}(\eta)}{\sqrt{\Var(\eta)}}=\sqrt{n}\gg 1.

This verify the conditions required for the martingale CLT as stated in Lemma 9.12 of
9
. Therefore, the appropriately scaled second-order approximation of our test statistic satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{T_{2}^{(S)}-\mathbb{E}[T_{2}^{(S)}]}{\sqrt{\Var(T_{2}^{(S)})}}\xrightarrow{D}\mathcal{N}(0,1)

which establishes the result.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

10

Proof.

The third order term is

\left(\langle{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top},S_{1,3}({X^{(1)}})\rangle+\langle{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top},S_{2,3}({X^{(2)}})\rangle+\sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=3}\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right).

The first two terms are directly
o\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)
using
Lemma

5
. For the mixed terms, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{X^{(2)}}}\!\left[\mathbb{E}_{{X^{(1)}}}\!\left(\left\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\right\rangle\,\big|\,{X^{(2)}}\right)\right]

(130)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{X^{(2)}}}\!\left[\left\langle\mathbb{E}_{{X^{(1)}}}\!\left(S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}})\right),S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=0.

By the same argument as in
Eq.

130
, the remaining mixed term

\mathbb{E}\langle S_{1,2}({X^{(1)}}),S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\rangle
also vanishes identically.
Indeed, since
{X^{(1)}}
and
{X^{(2)}}
are independent and centered, the inner
conditional expectation with respect to either random matrix is zero.
This completes the proof of
Lemma

10
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

11

Proof.

This proof follows by identical index–counting applied component-wise as in
Lemma

6
. Without loss of generality, take
l_{1}=1
and
l_{2}=2
. First we expand

\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle
:

\displaystyle\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle

\displaystyle=\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-2}+\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-2}{X^{(2)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\right)

(131)

\displaystyle-\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}+\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\right).

From the expansion in
Eq.

131
, we will establish that the term whose variance dominates the other terms and hence is the dominant variance contributor to

\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle,

arises from the fully separated monomials

\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-2}\right)
and
\tr\left(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-2}{X^{(2)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\right)
, and then calculate its order.
We first show that

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}})(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}})\beta_{2}^{-2}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}{X^{(2)}}^{2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\right)\right)}\to 1,

exactly paralleling the one–sample setting as in
Section

E.6
.

Consider the mixed trace

R_{3}:=\tr\big(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}({X^{(2)}})^{2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\big).

Decompose
{X^{(2)}}=\left({V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}+\beta^{\perp}\right){X^{(2)}}
in the definition of
R_{3}
.
The unique term in which all insertions equal
\beta^{\perp}
is the fully separated monomial

R_{4}:=\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}})(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}})\beta_{2}^{-2}\right),

the two–sample analogue of
R_{2}
in
Section

E.6
.
Every other summand (a remainder term) contains at least one factor
{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}
.
Fix a remainder term

\mathcal{N}:=\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}(N_{1}{X^{(2)}})(N_{2}{X^{(2)}})\beta_{2}^{-2}\right),

where each of
N_{1},N_{2}
is either
{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}
or
\beta^{\perp}
.
Expanding the trace into its index form, exactly as in
Section

E.6
, yields

\mathcal{N}=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\atop j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}c(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})\,({X^{(1)}})_{i_{1},j_{2}}\,({X^{(2)}})_{i_{2},j_{3}}\,({X^{(2)}})_{i_{3},j_{1}},

where

c(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}):=(\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-1})_{j_{1},i_{1}}\,(N_{1})_{j_{2},i_{2}}\,(N_{2})_{j_{3},i_{3}}.

Using the same arguments as in
Section

E.6
, we define

\Sigma(\mathcal{N}):=\sum_{(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})\sim(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})}c(\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j})c(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{l})
which enables us to write
\Var(\mathcal{N})\asymp\Sigma(\mathcal{N})\rho_{n}^{3}.

The exact same argument of
Section

E.6
holds here as well. Consider the case where
N_{1}=VV^{\top}
and
N_{2}=\beta^{\perp}
. The entrywise bounds for
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
implied by
4
yield
\Sigma(\mathcal{N})\lesssim\frac{k^{4}}{n^{5}\rho_{n}^{6}}
. Similarly, if
N_{1}=N_{2}=VV^{\top}
, we have
\Sigma(\mathcal{N})\lesssim\frac{k^{6}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{6}}
.
It follows that whenever at least one of
N_{1}
or
N_{2}
equals
VV^{\top}
, the variance of the monomial satisfies

\Var(\mathcal{N})=o(\Var(R_{4})),
as in
Eq.

105
.

In contrast, just like in
Eq.

106
,

\Var(R_{3})\asymp n^{2}\rho_{n}^{3}\|\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-1}\|_{F}^{2}\asymp\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{3}}.

Using the same logic as in
Eq.

107
, this immediately proves

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}})(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}})\beta_{2}^{-2}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}{X^{(2)}}^{2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\right)\right)}\to 1.

The same argument establishes

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)\right)}\to 1.

Thus, it remains to show

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\big(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}{X^{(2)}}^{2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\big)\right)}\to 0.

Write the trace in the numerator as

R_{2,\mathrm{num}}:=\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right).

Using the same argument employed in
Section

E.6
,

\displaystyle\Var(R_{2,\mathrm{num}})

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}^{3}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}\atop j_{1},j_{2}}\left[(\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-1})_{i_{1},j_{1}}\,(\beta_{2}^{-1})_{i_{2},j_{2}}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}^{3}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}\atop j_{1},j_{2}}\frac{k^{6}}{n^{10}\rho_{n}^{6}}\lesssim\frac{k^{6}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{3}}.

Thus,

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\big(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}{X^{(2)}}^{2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\big)\right)}=\frac{\Var(R_{2,\mathrm{num}})}{\Var(R_{3})}\lesssim\frac{\frac{k^{6}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{3}}}{\frac{k^{2}}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{3}}}\to 0.

Similarly we can show

\frac{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\right)\right)}{\Var\left(\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}})(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}})\beta_{2}^{-2}\right)\right)}\to 0.

So, in
Eq.

131
, the variance of the terms
\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-2}\right)
and
\tr\left(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-2}{X^{(2)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\right)
is of order
k^{2}/(n^{4}\rho_{n}^{3})
. The remaining terms of

\Var\!\big(\langle S_{1,1}(X),S_{2,2}(X)\rangle\big)

have variance of order

o\!\left(k^{2}/(n^{4}\rho_{n}^{3})\right)
.
Since the number of summands in

\langle S_{1,1}(X),S_{2,2}(X)\rangle

is finite, the claim of the lemma follows.
∎

Appendix G
Proofs of Lemmas from
Appendix

C

In this section we prove the additional results from
Appendix

C
.

Proof of
Lemma

12

Proof of
Lemma

12
.

Write
\mathcal{E}_{22}=\{\,\|\widehat{V}\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{k/n}\,\}
.
By union bound,

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{{\sf very\ good}}^{c})\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{{\sf good}}^{c})+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{22}^{c}).

The first term was controlled in
Lemma

1
, namely

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{{\sf good}}^{c})=O(n^{-19}).
To bound
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{22}^{c})
we invoke Lemma C.6 of
2
(where we have that
\theta_{i}\asymp\sqrt{\rho_{n}}
for all
n
as well as the fact that their result holds under the same assumptions we impose herein). Under
1
and
3
that lemma guarantees that, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-19})
, there exists an orthogonal alignment matrix
W_{*}
such that

\|\widehat{V}-VW_{*}\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n\rho_{n}}}\|V\|_{2,\infty}.

Combining this with the triangle inequality gives, with the same high probability,

\|\widehat{V}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|\widehat{V}-VW_{*}\|_{2,\infty}+\|V\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n\rho_{n}}}\Big)\|V\|_{2,\infty}.

4
implies
\|V\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{k/n}
and, since
\sqrt{\frac{\log n}{n\rho_{n}}}=o(1)
under
2
, the right-hand side is
\lesssim\sqrt{k/n}
. Hence

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{22}^{c})=O(n^{-19}).

Combining the two bounds yields

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{{\sf very\ good}})\geq 1-O(n^{-19}),

as claimed.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

13

Proof of
Lemma

13
.

We begin with the following identity for the difference between the true probability matrix and its rank-
k
approximation:

\displaystyle P-\widehat{A}=P-\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}P\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}(A-P)\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}.

Invoking Theorem 1 of
58
, we can expand the projection operator
\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}
as

\displaystyle\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}=VV^{\top}+\sum_{l\geq 1}S_{l}(X),

where the operators
S_{l}(X)
are defined in
Appendix

A
. Substituting this expansion, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{A}-P

\displaystyle=\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}P\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}+\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}X\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-P

(132)

\displaystyle=\big(VV^{\top}+\sum_{l\geq 1}S_{l}(X)\big)\,P\,\big(VV^{\top}+\sum_{l\geq 1}S_{l}(X)\big)+\big(VV^{\top}+\sum_{l\geq 1}S_{l}(X)\big)\,X\,\big(VV^{\top}+\sum_{l\geq 1}S_{l}(X)\big)-P.

Expanding and collecting terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{A}-P

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\geq 1}S_{l}(X)\,P+\sum_{l\geq 1}P\,S_{l}(X)+\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\geq 1}S_{l_{1}}(X)\,P\,S_{l_{2}}(X)

\displaystyle\quad+VV^{\top}XVV^{\top}+\sum_{l\geq 1}S_{l}(X)\,X\,VV^{\top}+\sum_{l\geq 1}VV^{\top}XS_{l}(X)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}\geq 1}S_{l_{1}}(X)\,X\,S_{l_{2}}(X).

From this decomposition, it follows that the first-order term (i.e., the term linear in
X
) is

\displaystyle T_{1}(X)=VV^{\top}X(I-VV^{\top})+(I-VV^{\top})XVV^{\top}+VV^{\top}XVV^{\top},

while for general
l\geq 2
,

\displaystyle T_{l}(X)

\displaystyle=S_{l}(X)\,P+P\,S_{l}(X)+\sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}S_{l_{1}}(X)\,P\,S_{l_{2}}(X)

\displaystyle+S_{P,l-1}(X)\,X\,VV^{\top}+XVV^{\top}S_{P,l-1}(X)

\displaystyle+\sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l-1}S_{l_{1}}(X)\,X\,S_{l_{2}}(X).

By Theorem 1 of
58
, we have the operator norm bound

\|S_{l}(X)\|\lesssim\left(\frac{\|X\|}{n\rho_{n}}\right)^{\!l}.

Combining this with the decomposition above, we obtain

\displaystyle\|T_{l}(X)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim(l+1)\,2^{\,l-1}\,(n\rho_{n})\,\|S_{l}(X)\|+l\,2^{\,l-2}\,\|X\|\,\|S_{l}(X)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim c_{l}\left(\frac{\|X\|}{n\rho_{n}}\right)^{\!l}\,(n\rho_{n}),

for constants
c_{l}>0
such that
\log c_{l}=O(1)
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

14

Proof of
Lemma

14
.

In this proof, we will assume that
A
(in the one-sample case) and
{A^{(i)}}
(in the two-sample case) both belong to
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf very\ good}}
, as defined in
Lemma

12
, which happens with high probability as already proved.

\displaystyle\tr\bigl(VV^{\top}\,\Delta M\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}(P_{ij}(1-P_{ij})-{\widehat{P}}_{ij}(1-{\widehat{P}}_{ij}))

(133)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}(P_{ij}-{\widehat{P}}_{ij})+\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}({\widehat{P}}_{ij}^{2}-P_{ij}^{2}).

We will start the proof by first proving that

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}(P_{ij}-{\widehat{P}}_{ij})\bigg|=\bigg|\tr\left(VV^{\top}\Diag(P-\widehat{P}\right)\bigg|=O_{p}(\max(k,k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n})).

(134)

By
Lemma

13
, we can expand
\Diag((\widehat{P}-P)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})
. We first use Bernstein’s inequality to bound the first–order term, and then control the combined contribution of higher–order terms.

Define

u:=VV^{\top}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n},y:=(I-VV^{\top})Xu
.
By
Lemma

13
, the term of
\Diag((\widehat{P}-P)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})
that is linear in
X
, is
\Diag(((I-VV^{\top})XVV^{\top}+XVV^{\top})\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})
. We will control the first term which is
\Diag((I-VV^{\top})XVV^{\top}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}))=\Diag(y)
. The bound for the second term
\Diag(XVV^{\top}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}))
follows in exactly the same way. Let
w:=\mathrm{diag}(VV^{\top})
and set
\alpha:=w^{\top}(I-VV^{\top})
. Then

\displaystyle\tau_{1}

\displaystyle:=\tr\bigl(VV^{\top}\Diag(y)\bigr)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(VV^{\top})_{ii}y_{i}=w^{\top}y=w^{\top}(I-VV^{\top})Xu=\alpha^{\top}Xu.

(135)

Since
X
is symmetric and centered with independent off–diagonal entries
{X_{ij}}
,
\tau_{1}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\tau_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\alpha_{i}{X_{ij}}u_{j}=\sum_{i<j}\epsilon_{ij}{X_{ij}}+\sum_{i}\epsilon_{ii}x_{ii},

(136)

where
\epsilon_{ii}:=\alpha_{i}u_{i}
and
\epsilon_{ij}:=\alpha_{i}u_{j}+\alpha_{j}u_{i}
. To bound
\epsilon_{ij}
and
\Var(\tau_{1})
, note that

\|w\|\lesssim kn^{-1/2},\quad\|\alpha\|=\|w(I-VV^{\top})\|\lesssim kn^{-1/2},\quad\|u\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{kn},\quad\|u\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{k}.

(137)

Next, we have
\|\alpha\|_{\infty}\leq\|w\|_{\infty}+\|w^{\top}VV^{\top}\|_{\infty}=O\left(\frac{k}{n}\right)
from
4
. Hence

|\epsilon_{ij}|\leq 2\|\alpha\|_{\infty}\|u\|_{\infty}=O(k^{2}n^{-1}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Var(\tau_{1})=\sum_{i\leq j}\epsilon_{ij}^{2}\,\Var({X_{ij}})\lesssim\sum_{i\leq j}(\alpha_{i}u_{j}+\alpha_{j}u_{i})^{2}\rho_{n}\lesssim k^{2}\rho_{n},

and moreover
\max_{i,j}|\epsilon_{ij}{X_{ij}}|\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n}
. By Bernstein’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\tau_{1}|>t)\lesssim\exp\Biggl(-\frac{t^{2}/2}{k^{2}\rho_{n}+k^{2}t/(3n)}\Biggr).

So by choosing
t\gtrsim k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}
, we have

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=O_{p}\left(k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n}\right).

(138)

This establishes the bound on the first–order term for
\tr(VV^{\top}\Delta M)
.

The higher–order contributions are given by

\sum_{l\geq 2}\tr\bigl(VV^{\top}\,\Diag(T_{l}(X)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})\bigr).

For each
l\geq 2
, we have

\tr\bigl(VV^{\top}\,\Diag(T_{l}(X)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})\bigr)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(VV^{\top})_{ii}\,\bigl(T_{l}(X)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}\bigr)_{i}\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{k}{n}\,\bigl(T_{l}(X)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}\bigr)_{i},

where the inequality follows from
4
. Now
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(T_{l}(X)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}\bigr)_{i}
is the sum of all entries in the vector
T_{l}(X)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}
. Thus
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(T_{l}(X)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}\bigr)_{i}=\langle\mathbf{1}_{n},(T_{l}(X)\mathbf{1}_{n})\rangle\leq n\|T_{l}(X)\|
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq 2}\tr\bigl(VV^{\top}\,\Diag(T_{l}(X)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})\bigr)\lesssim\frac{k}{n}\,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(T_{l}(X)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n}\bigr)_{i}\leq\sum_{l\geq 2}\|T_{l}(X)\|=O_{p}(k),

(139)

where the final bound follows directly from
Lemmas

1
and
13
, which control the operator norms of the higher–order terms in the expansion. Thus
Eqs.

138
and
139
together prove
Eq.

134
.

Next, we will bound the second term on the right hand side of
Eq.

133
by proving that

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}(P_{ij}^{2}-{\widehat{P}}_{ij}^{2})\bigg|=o_{p}(\max(k,k\sqrt{\rho_{n}\log n})).

(140)

We decompose the term further:

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}(P_{ij}^{2}-{\widehat{P}}_{ij}^{2})\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}P_{ij}(P_{ij}-\widehat{P}_{ij})-\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}(P_{ij}-\widehat{P}_{ij})^{2}\bigg|

(141)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}P_{ij}(P_{ij}-\widehat{P}_{ij})\bigg|+\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}(P_{ij}-\widehat{P}_{ij})^{2}\bigg|.

The second term on the right hand side of
Eq.

141
can be bounded using
Lemma

13
:

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}(P_{ij}-\widehat{P}_{ij})^{2}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}|(VV^{\top})_{ii}|\|P-\widehat{P}\|_{F}^{2}

(142)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{3/2}}{n}\|P-\widehat{P}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{p}(k^{3/2}\rho_{n})=o_{p}(k).

The first term on the right hand side of
Eq.

141
can be expressed as

\bigg|\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}P_{ij}(P_{ij}-\widehat{P}_{ij})\bigg|=\bigg|\tr(P\Diag(VV^{\top})(P-\widehat{P}))\bigg|.

We calculate its order in a manner very similar to what we did for the first term of
Eq.

133
earlier in the proof. By
Lemma

13
, we can expand
\Diag(\widehat{P}-P)
. We first use Bernstein’s inequality to bound the first-order term, and then control the combined contribution of higher-order terms:

\tr(P\Diag(VV^{\top})(P-\widehat{P}))=\tr(P\Diag(VV^{\top})T_{1}(X))+\sum_{l\geq 2}\tr(P\Diag(VV^{\top})T_{l}(X)).

Substituting
T_{1}(X)
from
Lemma

13
, we see that

\displaystyle\tau_{2}=\tr(P\Diag(VV^{\top})T_{1}(X))=\tr(P\Diag(VV^{\top})X)=\sum_{i,j}(VV^{\top})_{ii}P_{ij}X_{ij}.

(143)

We can easily check that
\Var(\tau_{2})\lesssim k^{2}\rho_{n}^{3}
from
1
and
4
and
|(VV^{\top})_{ii}P_{ij}X_{ij}|\leq\frac{k\rho_{n}}{n}
. By Bernstein’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\tau_{2}|>t)\lesssim\exp\Biggl(-\frac{t^{2}/2}{k^{2}\rho_{n}^{3}+k^{2}t\rho_{n}/(3n)}\Biggr).

Choosing
t\gtrsim k\sqrt{\rho_{n}^{3}\log n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\tau_{2}=O_{p}\left(k\sqrt{\rho_{n}^{3}\log n}\right)=o_{p}(k).

(144)

The higher-order terms can be bounded using
Lemmas

13
and
1
:

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq 2}\tr(P\Diag(VV^{\top})T_{l}(X))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l\geq 2}k^{3/2}\|P\|\|\Diag(VV^{\top})\|\|T_{l}(X)\|

(145)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sum_{l\geq 2}k^{5/2}c_{l}\left(\frac{\|X\|}{n\rho_{n}}\right)^{\!l}\,n\rho_{n}^{2}\right)=O_{p}\left(k^{5/2}\rho_{n}\right)=o_{p}(k).

Thus,
Eqs.

144
,
145
and
142
together prove
Eq.

140
. Since we have proved both
Eqs.

140
and
134
,
Eq.

40
also follows.

An identical argument applies to
Eq.

41
, where again we expand
\Delta\Sigma
and proceed analogously to the steps used for
Eq.

40
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

15

Proof of
Lemma

15
.

In this proof, we will assume that
A
(in the one-sample case) and
{A^{(i)}}
(in the two-sample case) both belong to
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf very\ good}},
as defined in
Lemma

12
, which happens with high probability as already proved.

Proof of
Eq.

42
.
Just as in the proof of
Eq.

40
, it suffices to show

\displaystyle\bigg|\tr\Bigl((V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top})\,\Diag({\widehat{P}})\Bigr)\bigg|=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right)\right),

(146)

because then we can similarly show that

\displaystyle\bigg|\tr\Bigl((V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top})\,\Diag({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})\Bigr)\bigg|=o_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right)\right).

To that end we first work with
D:=\Diag(P)
and prove

\bigg|\tr\Bigl((V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top})\,\Diag(P)\Bigr)\bigg|=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right)\right).

Using the algebraic identity

\displaystyle\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}=\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}+V\big(V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}-\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\widehat{V}\big)\widehat{V}^{\top}+V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\big),

(147)

and observing that

\displaystyle V\big(V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}-\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\widehat{V}\big)\widehat{V}^{\top}

\displaystyle=V\Lambda^{-2}\big(\Lambda^{2}V^{\top}\widehat{V}-V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{2}\big)\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}

\displaystyle=V\Lambda^{-2}\big(V^{\top}P^{2}\widehat{V}-V^{\top}A^{2}\widehat{V}\big)\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}

\displaystyle=V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big(P^{2}-A^{2}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}

\displaystyle=-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big(PX+XP+X^{2}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top},

we expand the leading (first–order) contributions using the series representation

\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}=S_{1}(X)+\sum_{j\geq 2}S_{j}(X),

with

S_{1}(X)=\beta^{\perp}X\beta^{-1}+\beta^{-1}X\beta^{\perp}.

Collecting the principal terms and using
Eq.

147
yields the following bound:

\displaystyle\bigg|\tr

\displaystyle\Bigl((\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top})\,\Diag(P)\Bigr)\bigg|

(148)

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\tr\Big(\big[V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{1}(X)+S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}-\,V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(XP+PX)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big]\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\tr\Big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(XP+PX)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\tr\Big(\big(\sum_{j\geq 2}V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)+\sum_{j\geq 2}S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-\,V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}X^{2}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\big)\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|.

The first term on the right hand side of
Eq.

148
corresponds precisely to those components in the expansion
Eq.

147
that are linear in the noise matrix
X
. We bound these contributions using a standard Bernstein–type concentration argument.

Consider first the term

\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{1}(X)\,\Diag(P)\right).

By direct expansion, this term can be written as

\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{1}(X)\,\Diag(P)\right)=\sum_{t,q=1}^{n}w_{tq}\,x_{tq}=\sum_{t>q}(w_{tq}+w_{qt})x_{tq}+\sum_{t}w_{tt}x_{tt},

where the deterministic weights
w_{tq}
are given by

w_{tq}=\sum_{s=1}^{k}\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{ss}^{3}}\,v_{rs}v_{ts}\,(I-VV^{\top})_{qr}\,p_{rr}.

Under
1
,
3
and
4
, these coefficients satisfy

|w_{tq}|\lesssim\bigg|\sum_{s=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\lambda_{ss}^{3}}\,v_{qs}v_{ts}\,p_{qq}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{2}}.

Moreover, since the entries
\{x_{tq}\}
are independent (up to symmetry) with variance of order
\rho_{n}
, we have

\Var\left(\sum_{t,q=1}^{n}w_{tq}x_{tq}\right)\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{3}}.

Applying Bernstein’s inequality yields, for any
z>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{t,q=1}^{n}w_{tq}x_{tq}\right|\geq z\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{z^{2}/2}{\frac{k^{2}}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{3}}+\frac{kz}{3n^{4}\rho_{n}^{2}}}\right).

Taking
z\asymp\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}
gives

\displaystyle\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{1}(X)\,\Diag(P)\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right).

(149)

A completely analogous argument applies to

\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}XV\Lambda^{-1}V^{\top}\,\Diag(P)\right),

which can again be written in the form

\sum_{t,q=1}^{n}w_{tq}x_{tq}=\sum_{t>q}(w_{tq}+w_{qt})x_{tq}+\sum_{t}w_{tt}x_{tt},

with coefficients

w_{tq}=\sum_{s,j=1}^{k}\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\lambda_{ss}^{2}\lambda_{jj}}\,v_{rs}v_{ts}v_{qj}v_{rj}\,p_{rr}.

Using the same approach one obtains

\displaystyle\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}XV\Lambda^{-1}V^{\top}\,\Diag(P)\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right).

(150)

The remaining linear terms,

\tr\left(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\,\Diag(P)\right)

and
\tr\left(V\Lambda^{-1}V^{\top}XV\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\,\Diag(P)\right),

are handled in exactly the same manner. Collecting all such contributions from
Eqs.

149
and
150
, we conclude that the total first–order (linear in
X
) component in
Eq.

148
is

O_{p}\left(\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right).

Bounding the final term in
Eq.

148
is straightforward. We first establish a high–probability bound for

\Bigg|\tr\Bigg(\sum_{j\geq 2}V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\,\Diag(P)\Bigg)\Bigg|.

By
Lemma

1
, on the corresponding high–probability event, we have

\|S_{j}(X)\|\lesssim\frac{1}{(n\rho_{n})^{j/2}},
for
j\geq 2.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\tr\Bigg(\sum_{j\geq 2}V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\,\Diag(P)\Bigg)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq 2}\Big|\tr\big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\,\Diag(P)\big)\Big|

(151)

\displaystyle\leq k\sum_{j\geq 2}\|\Diag(P)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\|\,\|S_{j}(X)\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sum_{j\geq 2}k\frac{\rho_{n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\,\frac{4^{j/2}}{(n\rho_{n})^{j/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

Applying an identical argument, we have

\Bigg|\tr\Bigg(\sum_{j\geq 2}S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\,\Diag(P)\Bigg)\Bigg|=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

Finally, consider the term

\tr\Big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}X^{2}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\,\Diag(P)\Big).

By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality for the Frobenius inner product,

\displaystyle\Big|\tr\!\Big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}X^{2}\,\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\,\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq k\,\big\|V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}X^{2}\big\|\,\big\|\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(P)\big\|

(152)

\displaystyle\leq k\,\|X\|^{2}\,\|V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\|\,\big\|\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(P)\big\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(k\,(n\rho_{n})\,\frac{1}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\,\frac{\rho_{n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right),

where the final bound follows from
Lemmas

12
,
3
and
1
. Combining the above estimates, we conclude that the last term on the right hand side of
Eq.

148
is of order

O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

We now turn to bounding the second term on the right hand side of
Eq.

148
. To this end, we once again decompose

\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}
using the expansion in
Eq.

147
. This yields

\displaystyle\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

(153)

\displaystyle+\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)V\big(V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}-\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\widehat{V}\big)\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\big)\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big(PX+XP+X^{2}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|.

All terms appearing on the right hand side of
Eq.

153
can be controlled using

1
,
2′
,
3
and
4
, together with the high–probability bounds established in
Lemma

12
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq 1}k\|S_{1}(X)\|\,\|S_{j}(X)\|\,\|\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(P)\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}k\frac{\rho_{n}}{n^{2.5}\rho_{n}^{2.5}}\,\frac{4^{j/2}}{(n\rho_{n})^{j/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right),

where the second line follows similarly as in
Eq.

151
. An identical bound holds for

\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|

by the same argument. Moreover,
Eq.

152
yields the same order of magnitude for

\Big|\tr\Big(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big(PX+XP+X^{2}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(P)\Big)\Big|.

Combining these bounds, we conclude that the second term on the right hand side of
Eq.

148
is of order

O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

By an entirely analogous argument, one can show that the third term on the right hand side of
Eq.

148
is also

O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

Specifically, one again decomposes

\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}
using
Eq.

147
and applies

1
,
2′
,
3
and
4
together with
Lemma

12
.
The resulting calculations mirror those used for the second term and therefore yield the stated bound.

We now turn to the deviation term involving
\Diag(\widehat{P})
. Observe that

\displaystyle\tr\bigl((\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top})\Diag(\widehat{P})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\tr\bigl((\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top})\Diag(P)\bigr)+\tr\bigl((\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top})(\Diag(\widehat{P}-P)\bigr)

(154)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right)\right)+\tr\bigl((\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top})\Diag({\widehat{P}}-P)\bigr),

where the bound for the first term follows from
Eq.

146
. It therefore suffices to control the second term. To this end, we invoke both the decomposition in
Eq.

147
and the series expansion in
Lemma

13
, and obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\tr\bigl((\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top})\Diag({\widehat{P}}-P)\bigr)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\tr\bigl((\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top})\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag({\widehat{P}}-P)\bigr)\Big|

(155)

\displaystyle+\Big|\tr\bigl(V\big(V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}-\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\widehat{V}\big)\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag({\widehat{P}}-P)\bigr)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\tr\bigl(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top})\Diag({\widehat{P}}-P)\bigr)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l\geq 1}\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big|\tr\bigl(S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(T_{l}(X))\bigr)\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{l\geq 1}\Big|\tr\bigl(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(PX+XP+X^{2})\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(T_{l}(X))\bigr)\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{l\geq 1}\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big|\tr\bigl(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\Diag(T_{l}(X))\bigr)\Big|.

We now derive high–probability bounds for the terms on the right hand side of
Eq.

155
. Using
1
,
2′
,
3
and
4
together with the event in
Lemma

12
and the series representation in
Lemma

13
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq 1}\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big|\tr\bigl(S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(T_{l}(X))\bigr)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq k\sum_{l,j\geq 1}\|S_{j}(X)\|\,\|\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Diag(T_{l}(X))\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sum_{l,j\geq 1}\frac{k}{(n\rho_{n})^{2}}\frac{4^{j/2}}{(n\rho_{n})^{j/2}}\frac{4^{l/2}}{(n\rho_{n})^{l/2}}\rho_{n}\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}\right).

The final inequality follows from the bound

(T_{l}(X))_{ij}\lesssim\rho_{n}\Big(\frac{\|X\|}{n\rho_{n}}\Big)^{l},

which holds by the definition of
T_{l}(X)
in
Lemma

13
. The remaining two terms in
Eq.

155
can be bounded in an identical manner by applying the same norm inequalities and high–probability controls. Combining all these bounds for the four terms in
Eq.

148
and
Eq.

154
completes the proof of
Eq.

146
.

Proof of
Eq.

43

This bound follows directly from
Eq.

42
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\big|\tr\bigl((V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top})\,\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{n})\bigr)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim n\,\big|\tr\bigl((V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}-\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top})\,\Diag(\widehat{\Sigma}\bigr)\big|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{k}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}},\frac{k\sqrt{\log n}}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right)\right),

which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

16

Proof of
Lemma

16
.

Just like in
Section

G.3
, we will assume that
A
(in the one-sample case) and
{A^{(i)}}
(in the two-sample case) both belong to
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf very\ good}},
as defined in
Lemma

12
, which happens with high probability as already proved.

Proof of
Eq.

44

We have

\alpha=\mathrm{diag}(\beta^{-2})

and

\widehat{\alpha}=\mathrm{diag}(\widehat{\beta}^{-2}).

Once again, we invoke
Eq.

147
to obtain the bound

\displaystyle\bigg\|

\displaystyle\mathrm{diag}\Bigl(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Bigr)\bigg\|

(156)

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\mathrm{diag}\Big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{1}(X)+S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(XP+PX)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle+\Big\|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle+\Big\|\mathrm{diag}\Big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(XP+PX)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle+\Big\|\mathrm{diag}\Big(\sum_{j\geq 2}V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)+\sum_{j\geq 2}S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}X^{2}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big)\Big\|.

Consider first the vector

r=\mathrm{diag}\left(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{1}(X)\right)=\mathrm{diag}\left(\beta^{-3}X\beta^{\perp}\right).

For each
i
,

r_{i}=u_{i}^{\top}\Lambda^{-3}w_{i},

where

u_{i}=V^{\top}e_{i}

and

w_{i}=V^{\top}X\beta^{\perp}e_{i}
.
By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

|r_{i}|^{2}\leq\|\Lambda^{-3}\|_{2}^{2}\|u_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|w_{i}\|_{2}^{2}.

Consequently,

\|r\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}r_{i}^{2}\leq\|\Lambda^{-3}\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|u_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|w_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{k}{n}\|\Lambda^{-3}\|_{2}^{2}\,\|V^{\top}X\beta^{\perp}\|_{F}^{2},

where the final inequality follows from the incoherence assumption in
4
.

Using standard norm inequalities,

\|V^{\top}X\beta^{\perp}\|_{F}\leq\|V\|_{F}\|X\|_{2}\|\beta^{\perp}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{k}\,\|X\|_{2}.

Therefore,

\|r\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{k}{\sqrt{n}}\|X\|_{2}\|\Lambda^{-3}\|_{2}.

On the event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf good}}
, together with the eigen-scaling assumption in
3
, we conclude that

\|r\|_{2}=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2.5}}\right).

Similarly we can also show that
\|\mathrm{diag}\left(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\right)\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2.5}}\right)
.

Next, consider the vector

r=\mathrm{diag}\left(\beta^{-2}X\beta^{-1}\right),

for which, for each
i
,

r_{i}=(V^{\top}e_{i})^{\top}\Lambda^{-2}(V^{\top}X\beta^{-1}e_{i}).

Proceeding exactly as in the previous argument, using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the incoherence condition in
4
, standard norm inequalities, the bound on event
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf good}}
as in
Lemma

1
, together with the eigenvalue scaling in
3
, we obtain

\|\mathrm{diag}(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}XPV\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top})\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2.5}}\right).

The same bound holds for

\|\mathrm{diag}(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}PXV\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top})\|.

Consequently, the entire first term on the right-hand side of
Eq.

156
is bounded by

O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2.5}}\right).

We now turn to bounding the second term in
Eq.

156
. To this end, we once again decompose

\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}
using the expansion in
Eq.

147
. This yields

\displaystyle\Big|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big)\Big|

(157)

\displaystyle+\Big|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)V\big(V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}-\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\widehat{V}\big)\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\big)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big(PX+XP+X^{2}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\Big)\Big|.

We first focus on the first term

r_{j}=\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)\,S_{j}(X)\,\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big).

For each
i
, we may write

(r_{1j})_{i}=u_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}w_{i},\qquad u_{i}:=e_{i}^{\top}S_{1}(X)S_{j}(X)\widehat{V},\quad w_{i}:=\widehat{V}^{\top}e_{i}.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

(r_{1j})_{i}^{2}\leq\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\|_{2}^{\,2}\,\|u_{i}\|_{2}^{\,2}\,\|w_{i}\|_{2}^{\,2}.

Summing over
i
and using the incoherence of
\widehat{V}
from
Lemma

12
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|r_{1j}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{n}}\,\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\|\,\|S_{1}(X)S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\|_{F}

(158)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{\sqrt{n}}\,\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\|\,\|S_{1}(X)S_{j}(X)\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{\sqrt{n}}\,\|\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\|\,\|S_{1}(X)\|\,\|S_{j}(X)\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{\sqrt{n}}\,\frac{1}{n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}}\left(\frac{4}{n\rho_{n}}\right)^{(j+1)/2}\right).

Summing the bound in
Eq.

158
over
j\geq 1
yields

\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big\|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)\,S_{j}(X)\,\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big)\Big\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3.5}\rho_{n}^{3}}\right).

Similarly, consider

r_{2j}=\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)\,V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\Big).

For each
i
, we may write

(r_{2j})_{i}=\big(e_{i}^{\top}S_{1}(X)\big)\,\big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\,\big(S_{j}(X)e_{i}\big).

Proceeding analogously to the previous case and invoking Cauchy–Schwarz inequality together with the incoherence of
V
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|r_{2j}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\Lambda^{-2}\|\,\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\|e_{i}^{\top}S_{1}(X)V\|_{2}\right)\,\|V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\|_{F}

(159)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\,\|\Lambda^{-2}\|\,\|V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\|_{F}\,\|S_{1}(X)V\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k}{n^{2.5}\rho_{n}^{2}}\,\|S_{1}(X)\|\,\|S_{j}(X)\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{2.5}\rho_{n}}\,\left(\frac{4}{n\rho_{n}}\right)^{(j+1)/2}\right).

Summing the bound in
Eq.

159
over
j\geq 1
yields

\sum_{j\geq 1}\Big\|\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)\,V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\Big)\Big\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3.5}\rho_{n}^{3}}\right).

A completely analogous argument applies to the middle term in
Eq.

157
,

\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{1}(X)\,V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big(PX+XP+X^{2}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big),

and yields the same rate. Consequently, the entire second term in
Eq.

157
is bounded by

O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3.5}\rho_{n}^{3}}\right).

By an entirely analogous argument, one can show that the third term in
Eq.

156
is also

O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{3/2}}{n^{3.5}\rho_{n}^{3}}\right).

Specifically, one again decomposes

\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}
using
Eq.

147
and applies

1
,
2′
,
3
and
4
together with
Lemma

12
.
The resulting calculations mirror those used for the second term and therefore yield the stated bound.

For the final term in
Eq.

156
, we first focus on

r_{3j}=\mathrm{diag}\Big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\Big).

For each
i
, we may write

(r_{3j})_{i}=u_{i}^{\top}\Lambda^{-2}w_{i},\qquad u_{i}=V^{\top}e_{i},\quad w_{i}=V^{\top}S_{j}(X)e_{i}.

Proceeding along the same lines as in
Eq.

158
, and using Cauchy–Schwarz inequality together with the incoherence of
V
, we obtain a bound on each
\|r_{3j}\|
. Summing over
j\geq 2
then yields

\sum_{j\geq 2}\|r_{3j}\|=O_{p}\!\left(\frac{k}{n^{3.5}\rho_{n}^{3}}\right).

The same argument applies to the remaining two terms in the final line of
Eq.

156
, namely

\sum_{j\geq 2}\mathrm{diag}\Big(S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{diag}\Big(V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}X^{2}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big),

and both admit the bound

O_{p}\!\left(\frac{k}{n^{3.5}\rho_{n}^{3}}\right).

Adding up the bounds obtained for each term in
Eq.

156
, we conclude that

\|\widehat{\alpha}-\alpha\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2.5}}\right),

which completes the proof of
Eq.

44
.

Proof of
Eq.

45
. From
Eq.

20
,

G=\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}+\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-1}

and

\widehat{G}=\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}+\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{G}\circ\widehat{G}-G\circ G\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\biggl\|\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\Bigl[\bigl(\widehat{\beta}_{i_{1}}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{i_{2}}^{-1}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\widehat{\beta}_{j_{1}}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{j_{2}}^{-1}\bigr)-\bigl(\beta_{i_{1}}^{-1}\beta_{i_{2}}^{-1}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\beta_{j_{1}}^{-1}\beta_{j_{2}}^{-1}\bigr)\Bigr]\biggr\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\bigl\|\bigl(\widehat{\beta}_{i_{1}}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{i_{2}}^{-1}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\widehat{\beta}_{j_{1}}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{j_{2}}^{-1}\bigr)-\bigl(\beta_{i_{1}}^{-1}\beta_{i_{2}}^{-1}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\beta_{j_{1}}^{-1}\beta_{j_{2}}^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\|_{F}.

We present the argument for one representative configuration
(i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2})=(1,2,1,2)
; all other cases follow identically. Then

\displaystyle\bigl\|\bigl(\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}\bigr)-\bigl(\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|\bigl(\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}-\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\|_{F}

(160)

\displaystyle+\bigl\|\bigl(\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}-\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\bigr)\circ\bigl({\beta}_{1}^{-1}{\beta}_{2}^{-1}\bigr)\bigr\|_{F}.

We begin by bounding the term

\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}-\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}.

By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}-\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}(\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}-\beta_{2}^{-1})\|_{F}+\|(\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}-\beta_{1}^{-1})\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\|_{F}\|\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}-\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}+\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}-\beta_{1}^{-1}\|_{F}\|\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}.

(161)

Since we know
\|\beta^{-1}_{2}\|_{2}\asymp\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}
and
\|\widehat{\beta}^{-1}_{1}\|_{2}\asymp\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}
from
3
, it suffices to derive a bound for the generic quantity

\|\widehat{\beta}^{-1}-\beta^{-1}\|_{F}.

To this end, we employ the identity

\displaystyle\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-1}V^{\top}

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\big)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}\widehat{V}^{\top}

(162)

\displaystyle\quad+V\big(V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}-\Lambda^{-1}V^{\top}\widehat{V}\big)\widehat{V}^{\top}

\displaystyle\quad+V\Lambda^{-1}V^{\top}\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\big),

which is a first–order analogue of
Eq.

147
. Observe that

\displaystyle V\big(V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}-\Lambda^{-1}V^{\top}\widehat{V}\big)\widehat{V}^{\top}

\displaystyle=V\Lambda^{-1}\big(\Lambda V^{\top}\widehat{V}-V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}\big)\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}\widehat{V}^{\top}

\displaystyle=-\,V\Lambda^{-1}V^{\top}X\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-1}\widehat{V}^{\top}.

Using
Eq.

162
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}^{-1}-\beta^{-1}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|_{F}\,\|\widehat{\beta}^{-1}\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\beta}^{-1}\|_{2}\,\|X\|_{2}\,\|\beta^{-1}\|_{F}

(163)

\displaystyle+\|\widehat{V}\widehat{V}^{\top}-VV^{\top}\|_{F}\,\|\beta^{-1}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{1/2}}{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}}\right),

where the final line follows from the Davis–Kahan bound in
Lemma

1
together with
3
.
3
also implies
\|\beta^{-1}\|_{2}\asymp\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}
and
\|\widehat{\beta}^{-1}\|_{2}\asymp\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}
.

Thus, substituting
Eq.

163
into
Section

G.5
yields

\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}-\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}\lesssim\frac{k^{1/2}}{n^{5/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}}.

Inserting this bound into
Eq.

160
and invoking
3
and
4
along with the incoherence of
\widehat{V}
, guaranteed by
Lemma

12
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\bigl\|(\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1})\circ(\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1})-(\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1})\circ(\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1})\bigr\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\!\left(\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}\|_{\infty},\|\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\right)\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}-\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{3/2}}{n^{11/2}\rho_{n}^{9/2}}.

This is because

\displaystyle\big|(\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1})_{ij}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|e_{i}^{\top}V_{1}\|_{2}\|\Lambda_{1}^{-1}\|_{2}\|V_{1}^{\top}V_{2}\|_{2}\|\Lambda_{2}^{-1}\|_{2}\|V_{2}^{\top}e_{j}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}.

Here, we used
3
and
4
to obtain the bound. Similarly, in order to provide the same bound for
\|\widehat{\beta}_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\beta}_{2}^{-1}\|_{\infty}
, we will use the incoherence of
\widehat{V}
, guaranteed by
Lemma

12
.

Summing over all index combinations yields the desired bound

\|\widehat{G}\circ\widehat{G}-G\circ G\|_{F}=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{3/2}}{n^{11/2}\rho_{n}^{9/2}}\right).

Proof of
Eq.

46
.

Once again, invoking
Eq.

147
yields

\displaystyle\bigg\|\widehat{V}

\displaystyle\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\bigg\|_{F}

(164)

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{1}(X)+S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(XP+PX)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Big\|_{F}

\displaystyle+\Big\|S_{1}(X)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Big\|_{F}

\displaystyle+\Big\|V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(XP+PX)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Big\|_{F}

\displaystyle+\Big\|\sum_{j\geq 2}V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)+\sum_{j\geq 2}S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}X^{2}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\Big\|_{F}.

By
1
and
3
, together with bound on
\|X\|_{2}
from
Lemma

1
and the fact that
\|S_{1}(X)\|_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}
, the first term on the right-hand side of
Eq.

164
satisfies

\displaystyle\Big\|V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{1}(X)+S_{1}(X)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(XP+PX)V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\Big\|_{F}=O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{k}}{n^{5/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}}\right).

(165)

The second and third terms can be bounded using submultiplicativity of the Frobenius norm and the operator-norm control of
S_{1}(X)
and
XP+PX
, yielding

\displaystyle\Big\|S_{1}(X)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Big\|_{F}+\Big\|V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}(XP+PX)\big(\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\big)\Big\|_{F}

(166)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}\bigg\|\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}-V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}\bigg\|_{F}.

Finally, for the last term in
Eq.

164
,
Lemma

1
implies

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{j\geq 2}V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}S_{j}(X)\Big\|_{F}=O_{p}\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3}}\right),

(167)

and the same bound holds for

\big\|\sum_{j\geq 2}S_{j}(X)\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\big\|_{F}

and

\big\|V\Lambda^{-2}V^{\top}X^{2}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Lambda}^{-2}\widehat{V}^{\top}\big\|_{F}
. Collecting
Eqs.

165
,
166
and
167
completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

17

Proof of
Lemma

17
.

Just like in
Section

G.4
, we will assume that
A
(in the one-sample case) and
{A^{(i)}}
(in the two-sample case) both belong to
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf very\ good}}
, as defined in
Lemma

12
, which happens with high probability as already proved.

Proof of
Eq.

47
.
We prove the claim directly. Using the triangle inequality we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\widehat{P}}^{2}-P^{2}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\widehat{P}}\|_{F}\|{\widehat{P}}-P\|_{2}+\|{\widehat{P}}-P\|_{2}\|P\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{1/2}\,n\rho_{n}\,\|X\|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(k^{1/2}\,n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}\right),

where the last inequality follows from
Lemmas

13
and
1
.

Proof of
Eq.

48
.

We begin by decomposing the difference as

{\widehat{P}}({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})-P(P\circ P)=({\widehat{P}}-P)({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})+P\bigl[({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})-(P\circ P)\bigr].

Note that
\|{\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}}\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i,j}\widehat{P}_{ij}^{4}=O_{p}\left(n^{2}\rho_{n}^{4}\right)
. Thus, for the first term on the right hand side,

\displaystyle\|({\widehat{P}}-P)({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq k^{1/2}\|{\widehat{P}}-P\|_{2}\,\|{\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(k^{1/2}\,n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}\right),

where the bound follows from
1
,
13
and
1
.

For the second term, observe that

\displaystyle\|({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})-(P\circ P)\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\|({\widehat{P}}-P)\circ({\widehat{P}}+P)\|_{F}

(168)

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{1/2}\|{\widehat{P}}-P\|_{2}\|{\widehat{P}}+P\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(k^{1/2}\,n^{1/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}\right).

Consequently,

\|P\bigl[({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})-(P\circ P)\bigr]\|_{F}=O_{p}\left(k^{1/2}\,n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{5/2}\right).

Combining the above bounds yields the claim of
Eq.

48
.

Proof of
Eq.

49
.

We again use the inequality

\displaystyle\|({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})^{2}-(P\circ P)^{2}\|_{F}\leq\|({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})-(P\circ P)\|_{F}\,\|({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})+(P\circ P)\|_{F}.

(169)

The first factor is bounded by
k^{1/2}\,n^{1/2}\rho_{n}^{3/2}
as shown in the last part,
while
1
and
3
imply

\|({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})+(P\circ P)\|_{F}=O_{p}\left(n\rho_{n}^{2}\right)
because of
\|{\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}}\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i,j}\widehat{P}_{ij}^{4}=O_{p}\left(n^{2}\rho_{n}^{4}\right)
as already shown in the last part.
Combining this bound with
Eqs.

168
and
169
, we obtain

\|({\widehat{P}}\circ{\widehat{P}})^{2}-(P\circ P)^{2}\|_{F}=O_{p}\left(k^{1/2}\,n^{3/2}\rho_{n}^{7/2}\right).

∎

Appendix H
Proofs of Lemmas from
Appendix

D

Proof of
Lemma

18

Proof.

Throughout this proof we assume that each
{A^{(i)}}
lies in the set
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf very\ good}}
of

Lemma

12
, which holds with high probability.
From Theorem 1 of
58
, we have

(\widehat{V}^{(i)})(\widehat{V}^{(i)})^{\top}-{V^{(i)}}{V^{(i)}}^{\top}=\sum_{l\geq 1}S_{i,l}({X^{(i)}}),

where
S_{i,l}({X^{(i)}})
is defined exactly as in
Eq.

37
.
Consequently, the second and third order terms of
{X^{(i)}}
in

\mathbb{E}\big\langle\widehat{V^{(1)}}\widehat{V^{(1)}}^{\top}-{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top},\widehat{V^{(2)}}\widehat{V^{(2)}}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\big\rangle

are

\mathbb{E}\left(\tr\big(S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}})S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\big)\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\left(\tr\big(S_{1,2}({X^{(1)}})S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})+S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}})S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\big)\right),

both of which vanish immediately, as shown in the proof of

Lemma

10
. Moreover, again from that proof, we have the bound

\displaystyle\big\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\big\rangle\leq\|S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}})\|_{F}\,\|S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\|_{F}=O_{p}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{(n\rho_{n})^{\,l/2}}\right),

(170)

where
l=l_{1}+l_{2}
.
Next, by
Lemma

8
and
Theorem

3
, it holds that

\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{2}\asymp k^{2}/(n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}).

We therefore obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\big\langle{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top},\widehat{V^{(2)}}\widehat{V^{(2)}}^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\displaystyle\sum_{l=2,3}\ \sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\big\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\big\rangle

(171)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq 4}\ \sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\big\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\big\rangle.

We will show that the second term in
Eq.

171
is
o_{p}(1)
under
2′
. This is because from
Eqs.

170
and
8
, we have the following bound with probability
1-O(n^{-c})
for a constant
c>1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\displaystyle\sum_{l\geq 4}\ \sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\big\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\big\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{n^{3/2}\rho_{n}}{k}\sum_{l\geq 4}\ 2^{l}\frac{k^{2}}{(n\rho_{n})^{\,l/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{1/2}\rho_{n}}=o_{p}(1).

For the first term on the right hand side of
Eq.

171
, we invoke Chebyshev’s inequality. As argued earlier,

\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{l=2,3}\ \sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\big\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\big\rangle\right)=0.

We therefore compute the order of

\Var\!\left(\sum_{l=2,3}\ \sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\big\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\big\rangle\right).

The first term in the above sum is

\big\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\big\rangle=\tr\!\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}+\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\right)=2\,\tr\!\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\right).

Hence it suffices to determine the order of

4\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\bigl(\tr(\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}})\bigr)^{2}\right]

since it is mean-zero.

Conditioning on
{X^{(1)}}
and writing
M_{1}:=\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{X^{(2)}}}\!\left[\bigl(\tr(M_{1}{X^{(2)}})\bigr)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i<j}\mathbb{E}\left(X^{(2)}_{ij}\right)^{2}\bigl((M_{1})_{ij}+(M_{1})_{ji}\bigr)^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(X^{(2)}_{ii}\right)^{2}(M_{1})^{2}_{ii}

(172)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(M_{1})_{ij}^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(M_{1})_{ij}(M_{1})_{ji}.

Taking expectation with respect to
{X^{(1)}}
, we will bound the first term on the right hand side of
Eq.

172
:

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}[(M_{1})_{ij}^{2}]

\displaystyle\asymp\rho_{n}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}[(M_{1})_{ij}^{2}]

(173)

\displaystyle=\rho_{n}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\bigl[\|M_{1}\|_{F}^{2}\bigr]=\rho_{n}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\bigl[\text{tr}(M_{1}M_{1}^{\top})\bigr].

For the second term of
Eq.

172
, we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the sum:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i\neq j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}[(M_{1})_{ij}(M_{1})_{ji}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\neq j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\bigl[|(M_{1})_{ij}||(M_{1})_{ji}|\bigr]

(174)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\neq j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\bigl[(M_{1})_{ij}^{2}+(M_{1})_{ji}^{2}\bigr]\lesssim\rho_{n}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\bigl[\text{tr}(M_{1}M_{1}^{\top})\bigr].

Thus,
Eqs.

173
and
174
imply that to find the order of
\mathbb{E}_{{X^{(2)}}}\!\left[\bigl(\tr(M_{1}{X^{(2)}})\bigr)^{2}\right]
as in
Eq.

172
, it suffices to evaluate that of
\rho_{n}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\bigl[\text{tr}(M_{1}M_{1}^{\top})\bigr]
.
To evaluate the expectation rigorously, note that both
\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}
and
\beta^{\perp}
are deterministic, symmetric, and positive semi-definite.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\!\left[\tr(M_{1}M_{1}^{\top})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\,\left(\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)_{ii}\,(\beta^{\perp})_{jj}+\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\,\left(\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)_{ij}\,(\beta^{\perp})_{ij}.

(175)

For the first term, since
\sigma_{ij}^{2}\asymp\rho_{n}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\left(\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)_{ii}(\beta^{\perp})_{jj}

\displaystyle\asymp\rho_{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\left(\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)_{ii}(\beta^{\perp})_{jj}

(176)

\displaystyle=\rho_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)_{ii}\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\beta^{\perp})_{jj}\right)

\displaystyle=\rho_{n}\,\tr\!\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\right)\,\tr(\beta^{\perp})

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3}}

It remains to show that the second term is smaller. By Cauchy–Schwarz,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\sigma_{ij}^{2}\left(\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)_{ij}(\beta^{\perp})_{ij}

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}|\left(\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)_{ij}|\,|(\beta^{\perp})_{ij}|

(177)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}\|\beta_{2}^{-1}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}\|_{F}\|\beta^{\perp}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{k}}{n^{7/2}\rho_{n}^{3}}

\displaystyle=o\!\left(\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3}}\right).

Therefore,

\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\!\left[\tr(M_{1}M_{1}^{\top})\right]\asymp\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3}}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\Var\Bigl(\bigl\langle S_{1,1}(X^{(1)}),S_{2,1}(X^{(2)})\bigr\rangle\Bigr)

\displaystyle=4\,\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl\{\tr(\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}X^{(1)}\beta^{\perp}X^{(2)})\bigr\}^{2}\Bigr]

(178)

\displaystyle\asymp\rho_{n}\,\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\!\left[\tr(M_{1}M_{1}^{\top})\right]

\displaystyle\asymp\rho_{n}\cdot\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{3}}=\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}.

We now proceed to bound

\operatorname{Var}\big(\langle S_{1,2}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\big)

using analogous arguments. We expand the inner product as

\begin{split}\langle S_{1,2}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\rangle&=\operatorname{tr}\left(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\right)\\
&\quad-\operatorname{tr}\left(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\right)\\
&\quad+\operatorname{tr}\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right)\\
&\quad-\operatorname{tr}\left(\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-1}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(2)}}\beta_{2}^{-1}\right).\end{split}

(179)

To analyze the variance, it suffices to determine the order of the second moment of the first term on the right hand side of
Eq.

179
because all other terms can be tackled in the same manner:

\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{tr}\left(\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}{X^{(2)}}\right)^{2}\right].

Conditioning on
{X^{(1)}}
as in
Eq.

172
and defining
N_{1}:=\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta^{\perp}{X^{(1)}}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{{X^{(2)}}}\left[\bigl(\operatorname{tr}(N_{1}{X^{(2)}})\bigr)^{2}\right]\asymp\sum_{i,j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(N_{1})_{ij}^{2}+\sum_{i\neq j}\sigma_{ij}^{2}(N_{1})_{ij}(N_{1})_{ji}.\end{split}

(180)

Taking expectation with respect to
X^{(1)}
, the same Cauchy–Schwarz argument used in

Eqs.

173
and
174
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\{\tr\left(\beta^{\perp}X^{(1)}\beta^{\perp}X^{(1)}\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-1}X^{(2)}\right)\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\asymp\rho_{n}\,\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\left[\tr(N_{1}N_{1}^{\top})\right]

(181)

\displaystyle\asymp\rho_{n}\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\left[\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2}X^{(1)}(\beta^{\perp}X^{(1)})^{3}\right)\right].

We now evaluate the last expectation. Expanding the trace gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1},\dots,i_{8}}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{1},i_{2}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{4}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{4},i_{5}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{5},i_{6}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{8}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{8},i_{1}}\Big].

The leading nonzero contributions arise from pairings of the four
X^{(1)}

entries into two equal unordered edge-pairs. Consider, for instance, the pairing

\displaystyle\{i_{2},i_{3}\}\sim\{i_{4},i_{5}\},\qquad\{i_{6},i_{7}\}\sim\{i_{8},i_{1}\}.

(182)

The contribution from this pairing is

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\dots,i_{8}\\
\{i_{2},i_{3}\}\sim\{i_{4},i_{5}\}\\
\{i_{6},i_{7}\}\sim\{i_{8},i_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}&\Bigg[(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{1},i_{2}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{4}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{4},i_{5}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{5},i_{6}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{8}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{8},i_{1}}\Bigg]\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\dots,i_{8}\\
\{i_{2},i_{3}\}\sim\{i_{4},i_{5}\}\\
\{i_{6},i_{7}\}\sim\{i_{8},i_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\Bigg[(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{1},i_{2}}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left((X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{2}}+(X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{3}}\right)}_{\text{Block 1}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{5},i_{6}}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left((X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{6}}+(X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{7}}\right)}_{\text{Block 2}}\Bigg].\end{split}

(183)

The cases
\{i_{2}=i_{5},\,i_{3}=i_{4},\,i_{6}=i_{8},\,i_{7}=i_{1}\}
and

\{i_{2}=i_{4},\,i_{3}=i_{5},\,i_{6}=i_{1},\,i_{7}=i_{8}\}
force
Eq.

183
to vanish, since

\sum_{i_{5},i_{6}}(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{1},i_{2}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{5},i_{6}}=0.

Thus the remaining pairings from
Eq.

182
are

\displaystyle\{i_{2}=i_{5},\,i_{3}=i_{4},\,i_{6}=i_{1},\,i_{7}=i_{8}\},

(184)

\displaystyle\{i_{2}=i_{4},\,i_{3}=i_{5},\,i_{6}=i_{8},\,i_{7}=i_{1}\}.

The two corresponding blocks in
Eq.

183
satisfy, uniformly in
i_{2}
and
i_{6}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{3}}\mathbb{E}\left[(X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}^{2}\left\{(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{2}}+(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{3}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{3}}\sigma_{i_{2}i_{3}}^{2}\left\{(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{2}}+(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{3}}\right\}

(185)

\displaystyle\asymp n\rho_{n},

and similarly

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{7}}\mathbb{E}\left[(X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}^{2}\left\{(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{6}}+(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{7}}\right\}\right]\asymp n\rho_{n}.

(186)

We also have the trace identity

\displaystyle\tr(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})\asymp\frac{k}{n^{6}\rho_{n}^{6}}.

(187)

Therefore, under the first pairing of
Eq.

184
, combining

Eqs.

183
,
185
,
186
and
187
we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\dots,i_{8}\\
\{i_{2},i_{3}\}\sim\{i_{4},i_{5}\}\\
\{i_{6},i_{7}\}\sim\{i_{8},i_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2}\right)_{i_{1},i_{2}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{4}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{4},i_{5}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{5},i_{6}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{8}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{8},i_{1}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}}\Bigg[(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{1},i_{2}}\sum_{i_{3}}\mathbb{E}\left((X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{2}}+(X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{2},i_{1}}\sum_{i_{7}}\mathbb{E}\left((X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{6}}+(X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{7}}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}\bigg|\sum_{i_{1}}\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2}\right)_{i_{1},i_{1}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}.

Similarly, under the second pairing of
Eq.

184
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\dots,i_{8}\\
\{i_{2},i_{3}\}\sim\{i_{4},i_{5}\}\\
\{i_{6},i_{7}\}\sim\{i_{8},i_{1}\}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2}\right)_{i_{1},i_{2}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{4}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{4},i_{5}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{5},i_{6}}

(188)

\displaystyle\times(X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{8}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{8},i_{1}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{6},i_{2}}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{3}}\mathbb{E}\left((\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{6}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{2}}+(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{6}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{2},i_{3}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{3}}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\sum_{i_{7}}\mathbb{E}\left((\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{7},i_{2}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{6}}+(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{7},i_{2}}(X^{(1)})_{i_{6},i_{7}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{7}}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i_{6},i_{2}}\left|\sum_{i_{3}}\sigma_{i_{2}i_{3}}^{2}(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{6}}\left\{(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{2}}+(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{3}}\right\}\right|\left|\sum_{i_{7}}\sigma_{i_{6}i_{7}}^{2}(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{7},i_{2}}\left\{(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{6}}+(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{7}}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i_{2},i_{6}}\rho_{n}^{2}\left(\sum_{i_{3}}\big|(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{6}}\big|\left(\big|(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{2}}\big|+\big|(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{3},i_{3}}\big|\right)\right)\left(\sum_{i_{7}}\big|(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{7},i_{2}}\big|\left((\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{6}}+(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{7}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho_{n}^{2}\sum_{i_{6},i_{2},i_{7}}\left|(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2})_{i_{7},i_{2}}\right|\left(|(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{6}}|+|(\beta^{\perp})_{i_{7},i_{7}}|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{2}\rho_{n}^{2}\left\|\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}.

The two other nonzero pairings,

\{i_{2},i_{3}\}\sim\{i_{6},i_{7}\},\qquad\{i_{4},i_{5}\}\sim\{i_{8},i_{1}\},

and

\{i_{2},i_{3}\}\sim\{i_{8},i_{1}\},\qquad\{i_{4},i_{5}\}\sim\{i_{6},i_{7}\},

are handled identically and have the same order. The configurations in which three or four
X^{(1)}
-entries collapse onto the same edge have fewer free indices and contribute at most

O\left(\frac{k}{n^{5}\rho_{n}^{5}}\right)=o\left(\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}\right),

Hence

\mathbb{E}_{X^{(1)}}\left[\tr\left(\beta_{1}^{-2}\beta_{2}^{-2}\beta_{1}^{-2}X^{(1)}(\beta^{\perp}X^{(1)})^{3}\right)\right]\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{4}}.

implying

\displaystyle\Var\big(\langle S_{1,2}(X^{(1)}),S_{2,1}(X^{(2)})\rangle\big)\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{3}}.

(189)

Similarly, we have

\operatorname{Var}\big(\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\big)\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{4}\rho_{n}^{3}}.

(190)

Using
Eqs.

178
,
189
and
190
and applying Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to the covariances, we bound the variance of the total sum:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}\left(\sum_{l=2,3}\ \sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=2,3}\ \sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\operatorname{Var}\left(\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\Cov\left(\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle,\langle S_{1,2}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\Cov\left(\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle,\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\Cov\left(\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\rangle,\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=2,3}\ \sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\operatorname{Var}\left(\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\Var\left(\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)\Var\left(\langle S_{1,2}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\Var\left(\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)\Var\left(\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\Var\left(\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,1}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)\Var\left(\langle S_{1,1}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,2}({X^{(2)}})\rangle\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k}{n^{3}\rho_{n}^{2}}.

Therefore, by Chebyshev’s inequality, we conclude

\frac{\sum_{l=2,3}\ \sum_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l}\langle S_{1,l_{1}}({X^{(1)}}),\,S_{2,l_{2}}({X^{(2)}})\rangle}{\widehat{\sigma}_{2}}\asymp 1,

which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

19

Proof.

Assume that
{A^{(i)}}
belongs to
\mathcal{E}_{{\sf very\ good}}
, as defined in
Lemma

12
, which happens with high probability as already proved.
Using the expansion in
Eq.

37
, we have

\displaystyle L(\Delta)

\displaystyle=\langle(\widehat{V}^{(1)})(\widehat{V}^{(1)})^{\top}-{V^{(1)}}{V^{(1)}}^{\top},\,\Delta\rangle-\langle(\widehat{V}^{(2)})(\widehat{V}^{(2)})^{\top}-{V^{(2)}}{V^{(2)}}^{\top},\,\Delta\rangle

(191)
